(You have not forgotten, Pepusch, that I myself am one of these magicians.) Well, I was not a little surprised at a communication from my colleague, which contained the most wonderful﻿—and at the same time the happiest﻿—intelligence of the princess that could be imagined. The matter was thus: by means of a scientific friend in Samarkand, my colleague had obtained the loveliest and rarest tulips, and as perfectly fresh as if they had been just cut from the stalk. His chief object was the microscopic examination of the interior portions, and in fact, of the petal. It was with this view that he was dissecting a beautiful tulip, and discovered in the cup a strange little kernel that struck him prodigiously, but how great was his astonishment when, on applying his glass, he perceived that the little kernel was nothing else than the Princess Gamaheh, who, pillowed in the petal of the tulip, seemed to slumber softly and calmly.

“However great the distance that separated me from my colleague, yet I set off immediately, and hastened to him. He had in the meantime put off all operations, to allow me the pleasure of a sight first, and perhaps, too, from the fear of spoiling something if he acted entirely from himself. I soon convinced myself of the perfect correctness of my colleague’s observations, and, like him, firmly believed that it were possible to snatch the princess from her sleep, and give her again her original form. The sublime spirit, dwelling within us, soon let us find the proper method, but as you, friend Pepusch, know very little﻿—in fact nothing at all﻿—of our art, it would be quite superfluous to describe to you the different operations which we went through to attain our object. It is sufficient if I tell you that by the dexterous use of various glasses﻿—for the most part prepared by myself﻿—we succeeded not only in drawing the princess uninjured from the flower, but in forwarding her growth, so that she soon attained her natural dimensions. Now, indeed, life was wanting, and this depended on the last and most difficult operations. We reflected her image by means of one of the best solar microscopes, and loosened it dexterously from the white wall, without the least injury. As soon as the shadow floated freely, it shot like lightning into the glass, which broke into a thousand shivers. The princess stood before us full of life and freshness. We shouted for joy, but so much the greater was our horror on perceiving that the circulation of the blood stopped precisely there where the Leech-Prince had fastened himself. She was just on the point of swooning, when we perceived on the very spot behind the left ear a little black dot, that quickly appeared and as quickly disappeared. Immediately the stagnation of the blood ceased, the princess revived, and our work had succeeded.

===Chinese===
“许多年过去了。两位术士吵翻了——学者之间常有这种事，而且学问越大吵得越勤——只是慑于习惯的铁腕，才仍把各自最重要的发现通报对方。（你没有忘记吧，佩普施，这两位术士里就有我一个。）喏，同僚来了一次通信，里面是关于公主的最奇特、同时也最可喜的消息，能想象得到的全在里面，我不由得大吃一惊。事情是这样的：同僚托撒马尔罕的一位学人朋友，得到了一批最可爱、最珍稀的郁金香，而且新鲜得很，仿佛才从花茎上剪下来一般。他的主要目的，是在显微镜下察看花朵的内部，确切地说，是那花瓣。正是抱着这个目的，他解剖一朵美丽的郁金香时，在花杯里发现了一粒古怪的小果仁，大为惊愕；可等他把镜片凑上去，他的惊愕又何其之大——他看出那粒小果仁不是别的，正是加玛赫公主，她枕在郁金香的花瓣里，仿佛睡得又轻又安稳。”

“尽管我和同僚之间隔着千山万水，我还是立刻动身，赶到他那里。他在此期间搁下了一切操作，好让我先享一睹的眼福——或许也是怕全凭自己动手会弄坏什么。我很快就确证，同僚的观察分毫不差，也同他一样坚信：把公主从沉睡中夺回、还她原本的形貌，是办得到的。我们心中居住的崇高灵性，很快就让我们找到了正法；不过，佩普施朋友，你对我们的技艺懂得极少——说实在的，一窍不通——把我们为达目的所经历的种种操作一一讲给你听，就纯属多余了。只需告诉你：凭着对各色镜片的巧妙运用——多半还是我亲手制备的——我们不但完好无损地把公主从花中取出，还催发了她的生长，使她不久就恢复了天然的身量。只是，生命还缺着，而这全系于最后、也最艰难的几道操作。我们用一架顶好的日光显微镜把她的影像映出来，再灵巧地从白墙上把它揭下，分毫无伤。那影子一获得自由，便像闪电似的射进镜片，镜片登时崩裂成千百碎片。公主站在我们面前，鲜活明媚。我们欢呼起来；可紧接着我们发现，血液循环恰恰停在水蛭王子当年吸附的那一处，我们的惊骇便加倍地大。她眼看就要晕厥，这时我们看见，她左耳正后方那个地方有一个小黑点，倏然出现，又倏然隐没。血液的阻滞应声消散，公主活了过来，我们的大功告成了。

===Original===
“Each of us﻿—that is, I and my colleague﻿—knew full well how invaluable was the possession of the princess, and each struggled for it, imagining that he had more right to it than the other. My colleague affirmed that the tulip, in which he had found the princess, was his property, and that he had made the first discovery, which he had imparted to me, and that I could only be deemed an assistant, who had no right to demand, as a reward of his labour, the work itself at which he had assisted. I, on the other hand, brought forward my invention of the last and most difficult process, which had restored the princess to life, and in the execution of which my colleague had only helped; so that, if he had any claims of propriety upon the embryo in the flower petal, yet the living person belonged to me. On this ground we quarrelled for many hours, till, having screamed ourselves hoarse, we at last came to a compromise. My colleague consigned the princess to me, in return for which I gave him an important glass, and this very glass is the cause of our present determined hostility. He affirms that I have treacherously purloined it﻿—an impudent falsehood﻿—and although I really know that the glass was lost in the transferring, yet I can declare, upon my honour and conscience, that I am not the cause of it, nor have I any idea how it could have happened. In fact, the glass is so small, that a grain of sand is about ten times larger. See, friend Pepusch: now I have told you all in confidence, and now you know that Dörtje Elverdink is none other than the revivified Princess Gamaheh, and must perceive that to such a high mysterious alliance a plain young man like you can have no﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我们两个——也就是我和我同僚——都深知占有公主是何等无价，于是各抢各的，都以为自己比对方更有权利。同僚一口咬定，发现公主的那朵郁金香是他的财产，头一个发现是他作出的，又是他透给我的，所以我至多算个帮工，没有权利把亲手帮着做成的这件活计本身讨去当工钱。我则搬出最后那道最艰难的工序是我的发明——公主还阳靠的正是它——而在这道工序里，他不过搭了把手；这么着，纵然他对花瓣里那枚胚芽还有什么名分上的权利，这活生生的人却属于我。在这个题目上，我们吵了足足好几个钟头，直到嗓子都喊哑了，才总算立下和约：同僚把公主让给我，作为回报，我给了他一片要紧的镜片。而正是这片镜片，成了我们如今势不两立的祸根。他一口咬定，是我使奸把它偷了去——无耻的谎话——虽说我的确知道，镜片是在交接的时候丢的，可我凭着我的荣誉和良心断言：那不是我干的，连它是怎么丢的，我也毫无头绪。这片镜片实在小得很，一粒沙子就比它大上十倍。瞧，佩普施朋友，如今我把一切全都悄悄告诉你了；现在你知道了，多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克不是别人，正是还阳的加玛赫公主；你也必定看得出，同这样高贵而神秘的姻缘，像你这样一位普普通通的年轻人，是不可能有——”

===Original===
“Stop!” interrupted George Pepusch, with a smile that was something satanic, “Stop! one confidence is worth another, and, therefore, I, on my side, will confide to you that I knew all that you have been telling me much earlier and much better than you did. I cannot laugh enough at your bigotry and your foolish pretensions. Know﻿—what you might have known long ago if your knowledge had not been confined to glass-grinding﻿—that I myself am the thistle, Zeherit, who stood where the princess had laid her head, and of whom you have thought fit to be silent through your whole history.”

===Chinese===
“且慢！”乔治·佩普施打断了他，笑容里带着几分撒旦气，“且慢！你交了一次底，也值得我交一次；所以在我这方面，我要悄悄告诉你：你方才对我讲的这一切，我知道得比你早得多，也清楚得多。你的那股执迷、你那套愚蠢的僭越，真叫人笑个没够。你可要知道——倘若你的见识不曾囿于磨镜片一行，这一点你早就该知道了——我自己就是那株蓟草精泽赫里特（the thistle, Zeherit），当初公主枕首的地方站着的正是我；而你讲这整段历史，偏偏自作主张把他瞒了个干净。

===Original===
“Pepusch!” cried the flea-tamer, “are you in your senses? The thistle, Zeherit, blooms in the distant Indies, in the beautiful valley, closed in by lofty rocks, where at times the wisest magi of the earth are wont to assemble: Lindhorst, the keeper of the records, can best inform you about it. And you, whom I have seen running about half starved with study and hunger, you pretend to be the thistle, Zeherit?”

“What a wise man you are, Leeuwenhoek!” said Pepusch, laughing: “Well, think of my person what you will, but do not be absurd enough to deny that, in the moment of the thistle Zeherit’s feeling the sweet breath of Gamaheh, he bloomed in glowing love and passion, and that, when he touched the temples of the sleeping princess, she too dreamt sweetly of love. Too late the Thistle perceived the Leech-Prince, whom he else had killed with his thorns in a moment, but yet, with the help of the root, Mandragora, he would have succeeded in recalling the princess to life, if the stupid genius, Thetel, had not interfered with his awkward remedies. It is true that in his passion the genius put his hand into the saltbox, which he is used to carry at his girdle when he travels, like Pantagruel, and flung a good handful at the Leech-Prince. But it is quite false that he killed him in so doing. All the salt fell into the marsh; not a single grain hit the prince, whom the thistle, Zeherit, slew with his thorns, and having thus avenged the murder of Gamaheh, devoted himself to death. It is the genius only﻿—who interfered in matters not concerning him﻿—that is the cause of the princess lying so long in the sleep of flowers; the Thistle awoke much earlier, for the death of both was but the same sleep, from which they revived, although in other forms. You will have completed the measure of your gross blunders, if you suppose that the Princess Gamaheh was formed exactly as Dörtje Elverdink now is, and that it is you who restored her to life. It happened to you, my good Leeuwenhoek, as it did to the awkward servant in the remarkable story of the Three Pomegranates; he freed two maidens from the fruit, without having first assured himself of the means of keeping them in life, and in consequence saw them perish miserably before his eyes. Not you, but *he*, who has escaped from you, whose loss you so deeply feel and lament; he it was who completed the work, which you began so awkwardly.”

===Chinese===
“佩普施！”驯蚤人喊道，“你脑子还清楚吗？蓟草精泽赫里特开花在遥远的东印度，在那座高崖环抱的美丽山谷里，世上最有智慧的术士们时不常在那里聚齐——这一点，档案保管人林德霍斯特（Lindhorst）最能讲给你听。而你——我是亲眼见过的，你抱着学问、饿着肚子，半死不活地满处跑——竟敢自称是蓟草精泽赫里特？”

“你可真是个明白人，列文虎克！”佩普施笑道，“好吧，随你怎样看我这个人，可你千万别荒唐到连这个也想否认：蓟草精泽赫里特感受到加玛赫甜美气息的那一瞬，他曾在炽烈的爱与情热里开花；他触到沉睡公主的鬓角时，她也在梦里做了爱情的甜梦。蓟草发现水蛭王子，已经太迟了——不然他一眨眼就能用刺把他扎死；可若不是那个蠢精灵忒特尔拿他那笨手笨脚的方子横加阻挠，他本可以靠着曼德拉草根，把公主重新唤回生命。不错，精灵盛怒之下确实把手伸进了盐盒——他出门旅行时，照例像潘塔格吕埃（Pantagruel）一样把盐盒系在腰带上——抓起好好一把朝水蛭王子扔去。可说他这样就结果了王子，纯属捏造。盐统统落进了沼泽，一粒也没有沾着王子；王子是蓟草精泽赫里特用刺杀死的。他报了加玛赫被杀之仇，随后便甘愿就死。公主之所以在花眠中沉睡那么久，全怪那个精灵——偏偏是他，插手了一件与他毫不相干的事；蓟草精倒是早就醒了，因为他们两个的死不过是同一场睡眠，从这场睡眠里他们各自还了魂，只是换了别的形骸。你若以为加玛赫公主生得就跟如今的多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克一模一样，以为让她还阳的是你，那你这一错，可就把粗疏的大错错到家了。我好心的列文虎克，你的遭遇，同《三颗石榴》（the Three Pomegranates）那个奇特故事里笨手笨脚的仆人一模一样：他把两位少女从果子里放了出来，却没有先弄清楚靠什么让她们活下去，结果眼睁睁看着她们惨死在自己面前。完成这件工程的不是你，而是*他*——那个从你身边逃走、你正为他的失去痛心哀叹的人；你笨手笨脚起了个头，是他替你做完了。

===Original===
“Ha!” cried the flea-tamer, quite beside himself, “Ha! ’twas so I suspected! But you, Pepusch, you, to whom I have shown so much kindness, you are my worst enemy: I see it well now. Instead of advising me, instead of assisting me in my misfortunes, you amuse me with all manner of nonsensical stories.”

“Nonsense yourself!” cried Pepusch, quite indignant. “You’ll rue your folly too late, you dreaming charlatan! I go to seek Dörtje Elverdink﻿—but that you may no longer mislead honest people﻿—”

He grasped at the screw which set all the microscopic machinery in motion﻿—

“Take my life at the same time!” roared the flea-tamer, but at the instant all crashed together, and he fell senseless to the ground.

===Chinese===
“哈！”驯蚤人喊道，整个人全然失态，“哈！我早就疑心是这么回事！可你，佩普施——你，我这个待你恩义不薄的人——你竟是我最凶的仇敌：这下我看明白了。你不替我出主意，不在我遭难的时候帮我一把，反倒拿种种荒唐故事来消遣我！”

“你才荒唐！”佩普施愤慨已极，喊道，“你这做梦的江湖骗子，你会追悔莫及的！我这就去找多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克——不过，为了叫你再也不能坑害老实人——”

他一把抓住那颗能开动整套显微镜机械的螺旋钮——

“索性连我的命也一并拿去！”驯蚤人吼道。可就在这一瞬，机器轰然塌作一团，他栽倒在地，不省人事。

===Original===
“How is it,” said George Pepusch to himself, when he had got into the street, “How is it that one, who has the command of a nice warm chamber and a well-stuffed bed, wanders through the streets at night in the rain and storm? Because he has forgotten the house key, and he is driven moreover by love.”

He could answer himself not otherwise, and indeed his whole conduct seemed silly in his own estimation. He remembered the moment when he saw Dörtje Elverdink for the first time. Some years before the Flea-tamer had exhibited his arts in Berlin, and had found no slight audiences as long as the thing was new. Soon, however, people had seen enough of the educated and well-disciplined fleas, and even the paraphernalia of the diminutive race began not to be thought so very wonderful, although at first attributed almost to magic, and Leeuwenhoek seemed to have fallen into total oblivion. On a sudden, a report was spread that a niece of the artist, who had not appeared before, now attended the exhibitions﻿—a beautiful, lovely little maiden, and withal so strangely attired as to baffle description. The world of fashionables﻿—who, like leaders in a concert, are accustomed to give the time and tune to society﻿—now poured in, and as in this world everything is in extremes, the niece excited unparalleled astonishment. It soon became the mode to frequent the flea-tamer; he who had not seen his niece could not join in the common talk, and thus the artist was saved in his distress. As to the rest, no one could comprehend the name “*Dörtje*;” and as at this time a celebrated actress was displaying, in the part of the Queen of Golconda, all those high yet soft attractions which are peculiar to the sex, they called the fair Hollander by the royal name, Alina.

===Chinese===
“这是怎么回事？”乔治·佩普施走到街上，自言自语道，“一个人，明明有暖暖和和的好屋子、厚厚实实的软床可供享用，竟会深更半夜冒着风雨在街上逛荡？因为他忘了带家里的钥匙，再说，爱情还在后头撵着他。”

他只能这样回答自己，而且在他自己的估价里，他这整个行径都显得傻气。他想起第一次见到多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克的那个时刻。几年前，驯蚤人曾在柏林献艺，趁那股新鲜劲儿没过，看客着实不少。可是不久，人们就把这些受过调教、训练有素的跳蚤看够了，连这一小人国的行头家什，也不大被当作多么了不得的奇迹了——虽然起初几乎被归功于魔法——列文虎克似乎已被忘了个干净。忽然有传言说，艺人有位从前从未露面的外甥女，如今来看演出了：一位美丽可爱的小姑娘，而且那身装束古怪得叫人无从描摹。时髦人物的世界——他们好比音乐会里的领奏，惯于给整个社交界定拍子定调子——顿时蜂拥而至；而这个世界里事事趋于极端，外甥女便激起了无与伦比的轰动。不久，去看驯蚤人成了风尚；谁没有见过他的外甥女，谁就插不进大家的谈资，艺人就这样在困顿中得救了。至于别的，谁也念不惯“多尔特耶”这个名字；恰逢其时，一位名伶正在《戈尔康达女王》一剧中，施展女性独有的那种高贵而又温柔的魔力，人们便拿这位女王的名字，称呼这位美丽的荷兰姑娘：阿莉娜。

===Original===
When George Pepusch came to Berlin, Leeuwenhoek’s fair niece was the talk of the day, and hence at the table of the hotel, where he lodged, scarcely anything else was spoken of but the little wonder that delighted all the men, young and old, and even the women themselves. Everyone pressed the newcomer to place himself on the pinnacle of the existing mode at Berlin, and see the Hollandress. Pepusch had an irritable, melancholy temperament; in every enjoyment he found too much of the bitter aftertaste, which indeed comes from the Stygian brook that runs through our whole life, and this made him gloomy and often unjust to all about him. It may be easily supposed that in this mood he was little inclined to run about after pretty girls, but he went nevertheless to the flea-tamer’s, less on account of the dangerous wonder than to confirm his preconceived opinion that here too, as so often in life, a strange madness was predominating. He found the Hollandress fair, indeed, and agreeable, but in considering her, he could not help smiling with self-satisfaction at his own sagacity, by the help of which he had already guessed that the heads, which the little one had so perfectly turned, must have been tolerably crazy before they left home.

The maiden had that light easy manner which evinces the best education; a mistress of that delightful coquetry, which, when it offers the fingertips to anyone, at the same time takes from him the power of receiving them, the lovely little creature knew how to attract her numerous visitors, as well as to restrain them within the bounds of the strictest decorum.

None troubled themselves about the stranger, who had leisure enough to observe all the actions of the fair one. But while he continued staring more and more at the beautiful face, there awoke in the deepest recesses of his mind a dark recollection, as if he had somewhere before seen the Hollandress, although in other relations and in other attire, and that he himself had at one time worn a very different form. In vain he tormented himself to bring this recollection to any clearness, yet still the idea of his having really seen the little creature before became more and more determinate. The blood mounted into his face, when at last someone gently jogged him, and whispered in his ear, “The lightning has struck you too, Mr. Philosopher, has it not?” It was his neighbour of the ordinary, to whom he had asserted that the ecstasy into which all had fallen was no better than madness, which would pass away as quickly as it had arisen.

===Chinese===
乔治·佩普施来到柏林时，列文虎克那位美丽的外甥女正是众口的谈资；因此在他下榻的旅馆饭桌上，大家谈来谈去，几乎离不开这位令老少须眉、连女人们自己都为之倾倒的小小奇迹。人人撺掇新来的客人，去登上柏林时下风尚的顶峰，去看看那位荷兰美人。佩普施性情暴躁而忧郁；在每一份享乐里，他都尝出太多苦涩的余味——这苦味其实发源于那条流贯我们一生的冥河之溪——这使他郁郁寡欢，也常常叫他待身边所有的人不公道。不难设想，怀着这样的心绪，他是不大乐意去追漂亮姑娘的；可他仍旧去了驯蚤人那里，倒不是为了那桩危险的奇观，更多是为了印证他的成见：这里也同人生里许多场合一样，是一种莫名其妙的痴狂在作祟。他发现这位荷兰美人确实又美又惹人喜欢；可他打量她的时候，忍不住自鸣得意地微微一笑，佩服自己的先见之明——多亏它，他早猜到了：被这小妮子迷得团团转的那些脑袋，出门之前八成就已经相当不正常了。

姑娘有着那种最能见出良好教养的轻盈随和的举止；她又精通那种妙不可言的卖俏——这种卖俏把指尖递给谁，同时也夺走了谁接住这指尖的本事——这位可爱的小人儿懂得怎样把她为数众多的访客吸引过来，又懂得怎样把他们约束在最严格的礼数之内。

谁也不来理会这位生客，他也就有的是工夫，把美人的一举一动看在眼里。可他越盯着那张美丽的脸看，他心灵最深处便醒来越发幽暗的一段回忆，仿佛他从前在什么地方见过这位荷兰美人，虽然是在别的关系、别的装束之中；又仿佛他自己当年也曾有一副全然不同的形骸。他苦苦折磨自己，要把这段回忆理出个眉目，终归徒劳；然而，“确实从前见过这小东西”的念头，反倒越来越确凿。血直往他脸上涌。这时终于有人轻轻碰了碰他，凑着他耳朵低语：“闪电也劈着你了吧，哲学家先生，是不是？”说话的是他同桌的邻座；他曾向这人断言，众人这种如痴如狂，不过是发了疯，来得多快，也会去得多快。

===Original===
Pepusch observed that while he had been gazing so fixedly on the little one, the hall had grown deserted. Now for the first time she seemed to be aware of his presence, and greeted him with graceful familiarity. From this time he could not get rid of her idea; he tormented himself through a sleepless night, only to come upon the trace of a recollection﻿—but in vain. The sight of the fair one, he rightly thought, could alone bring him to it, and the next day, and all the following days, he never omitted visiting the flea-tamer, and staring two or three hours together at the beautiful Dörtje Elverdink.

When a man cannot get rid of the idea of a beautiful woman who has riveted his attention, he has already made the first step towards love, and thus it happened that, at the very time Pepusch fancied he was only poring upon that faint recollection, he was already in love with the fair Hollandress.

Who would now trouble himself about the fleas, over whom Alina had gained so splendid a victory, attracting all within her own circle? The master himself felt that he was playing a somewhat silly part with his insects; he, therefore, locked up the whole troop for other times, and with much dexterity gave to his play another form, in which his niece played the principal character. He had hit upon the happy thought of giving evening entertainments, at a tolerably high rate of subscription, in which, after he had exhibited a few optical illusions, the further amusement of the company rested with his niece. Here the social talents of the fair one shone in full measure, and she took advantage of the least pause in the entertainment to give a new impulse to the party by songs, which she herself accompanied on the guitar. Her voice was not powerful; her manner was not imposing, often even against rule, but the sweetness and clearness of tone completely answered to her appearance, and when from her dark eyelashes she darted the soft glances, like gentle moonbeams, amongst the spectators, every breast heaved, and the censure of the most confirmed pedant was silenced.

===Chinese===
佩普施这才发觉，就在他死死盯着小姑娘看的当儿，大厅早已空了。她似乎这时才头一回觉察到他的存在，带着优雅的熟络向他问好。从这一刻起，他再也摆脱不掉她的念头；他熬过一个不眠之夜，只为追上一段回忆的踪迹——只是徒劳。他想得很对：只有再见到那位美人，才能把他引到那踪迹上去。于是第二天，以及此后的每一天，他从不错过去驯蚤人那里，一连两三个钟头地凝望美丽的多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克。

一个男人若是摆脱不掉一位攫住了他全部注意的美丽女子的念头，那么他已经在爱情之路上迈出了第一步。事情也正是这样：就在佩普施自以为只是苦苦追索那一段朦胧回忆的时候，他早已爱上了那位美丽的荷兰姑娘。

如今谁还肯为跳蚤操心呢？阿莉娜早已大获全胜，把一切人都拉进了她自己的圈子。主人自己也觉出，同他的虫豸们一起配戏，演的是一个相当愚蠢的角色；于是他把全军锁起来留待他日，又很巧妙地给他的戏换了另一种形式，由他的外甥女担纲主演。他想起了一个妙主意：举办晚间聚会，会费定得相当可观；他先展示几套光影幻术，随后的余兴，便全交给外甥女。美人的社交才华在这里发挥得淋漓尽致；她总能在演艺间隙里逮住哪怕最小的停顿，用歌曲给全场注入新的兴头，还亲自用吉他伴奏。她的嗓音并不洪亮，气派也不逼人，往往还失了章法，可音色的甜润清亮，同她的容貌恰恰相称；当她从乌黑的睫毛底下，把柔和的目光像溶溶月色一样投向四座，人人都胸口起伏，连最顽固的老学究也噤了责难之口。

===Original===
Pepusch diligently prosecuted his studies in these evening entertainments, that is, he stared for two hours together at the Hollandress, and then left the hall with the rest of the company. Once he stood nearer to her than usual, and distinctly heard her saying to a young man, “Tell me, who is that lifeless spectre, that every evening stares at me for hours, and then disappears without a syllable?”

Pepusch was deeply hurt, and made such a clamour in his chamber, and acted so wildly, that no friend could have recognized him in his mad freaks. He swore, high and low, never again to see the malicious Hollandress, but for all that, did not fail appearing at Leeuwenhoek’s on the very next evening, at the usual hour, to stare at the lovely Dörtje more fixedly if that were possible, than ever. It is true, indeed, that even upon the steps he was mightily alarmed at finding himself there, and in all haste adopted the wise resolution of keeping quite at a distance from the fascinating creature. He even carried this plan into effect by creeping into a corner of the hall, but the attempt to cast down his eyes failed entirely, and as before said, he gazed on the Hollandress more determinedly than ever. Yet he did not know how it happened that on a sudden Dörtje Elverdink was standing in his corner close beside him. With a voice that was melody itself, the fair one said, “I do not remember, sir, having seen you anywhere before our meeting here at Berlin, and yet I find in your features, in all your manner, so much that seems familiar. Nay, it is as if in times long past we had been very intimate, but in a distant country and in other relations. I entreat you: free me from this uncertainty, and if I am not deceived by some resemblance, let us renew the friendship, which floats in dim recollection like some delightful dream.”

===Chinese===
在这些晚间聚会上，佩普施勤奋地修他的功课——也就是说，一连两个钟头目不转睛地盯着那位荷兰美人，然后随众人一同离席。有一回，他站得比平常离她更近，清清楚楚听见她对一个年轻人说：“告诉我，那个死气沉沉的幽灵是谁？他每天晚上盯着我一盯就是几个钟头，然后一个字不留就没影儿了。”

佩普施受了深深的创伤。他在自己房里大吵大嚷，举止狂乱，闹得任何一个朋友都认不出他这副疯态了。他指天誓日，赌咒再也不去见那狠心的荷兰美人；话虽如此，就在第二天傍晚惯常的钟点，他照旧出现在列文虎克家里，只把可爱的多尔特耶盯得比以往更紧——倘若这还有可能的话。说实话，就连站在台阶上，他发觉自己居然又到了这里，也大大吓了一跳，急急忙忙立下一条英明的决断：离这迷人的造物远远的。他甚至当真把这计划付诸实行——悄悄爬进大厅的一个角落；可要把眼睛垂下去的尝试全盘失败；就像方才说的，他把那位荷兰美人盯得比先前更死了。然而他自己也不明白是怎么回事，忽然之间，多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克已经站到他那个角落里，紧挨着他了。美人开口了，那声音本身就是一支歌：“先生，我不记得在柏林这里相遇之前，曾在什么地方见过您；可我在您的容貌里、在您全部的举止里，却发现那么多似曾相识的东西。不，这就仿佛在久远的往昔，我们曾非常亲密，只是在一处遥远的国度里，又是另一种关系。我求求您：把我从这份疑惑里解救出来吧；倘若我不是被什么相貌的相似骗了，那就让我们重续这段交情——它在朦胧的回忆里浮动，像一场美妙的梦。”

===Original===
George Pepusch felt strangely at this address; his breast heaved, his forehead glowed, and a shudder ran through all his limbs as if he had lain in a violent fever. Though this might mean nothing else than that he was head over ears in love, yet there was another cause for this perturbation, which robbed him of all speech, and almost of his senses. When Dörtje Elverdink spoke of her belief that she had known him long before, it seemed to him as if another image was presented to his inward mind as in a magic lantern, and he perceived a long removed **self**, which lay far back in time. The idea, that by much meditation had assumed a clear and firm shape, flashed up in this moment, and this was nothing less than that Dörtje Elverdink was the Princess Gamaheh, daughter of King Sekakis, whom he had loved in a remote period, when he flourished as the thistle, Zeherit. It was well that he did not communicate this fancy to other folks, as he would most probably have been reckoned mad, and confined as such, although the fixed idea of a partial maniac may often, perhaps, be nothing more than the illusions of a preceding existence.

===Chinese===
听了这番话，乔治·佩普施心头泛起一种奇异的感觉；他胸口起伏，额头滚烫，一阵战栗窜过四肢，仿佛发过一场大热病。虽说这无非意味着，他已爱得神魂颠倒；可这番激荡还有另一重缘由——它剥夺了他全部的言语，几乎也剥夺了他的神志。当多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克说起她相信自己早已认识他时，他觉得仿佛有另一幅影像，像幻灯一般映上了他内在的心眼，他认出了一个久已远逝的**自我**，它躺在时间深处的远方。那个经过反复沉思、已经获得清晰而坚定形态的念头，此刻倏然亮起——这念头不是别的，正是：多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克就是加玛赫公主，塞卡基斯王的女儿，是他在远古的时代——当他作为蓟草精泽赫里特繁花盛放的时候——所爱的那一位。幸亏他没有把这个奇想讲给旁人听，否则十有八九要被当成疯子，当作疯子关起来；虽说一个偏执疯人的固定念头，说不定往往不过是前世的种种幻影罢了。

===Original===
“Good God! you seem dumb, sir!” said the little one, touching George’s breast with the prettiest finger imaginable, and from the tip of it shot an electric spark into his heart, and he awoke from his stupefaction. He seized her hand in a perfect ecstasy, covered it with burning kisses, and exclaimed, “Heavenly, angelic creature!” etc., etc., etc. The kind reader will easily imagine all that George Pepusch would exclaim in a such a moment. It is sufficient to say, that she received his love-protests as kindly as could be wished, and that the fateful moment, in the corner of Leeuwenhoek’s hall, brought forth a love affair that first raised the good George Pepusch up to heaven, and then again plunged him into hell. As he happened to be of a melancholy temperament, and withal pettish and suspicious, Dörtje’s conduct could not fail of giving rise to many little jealousies. Now it was precisely these jealousies that tickled Dörtje’s malicious humour, and it was her delight to torment the poor George Pepusch in a variety of ways, but as everything can be carried only to a certain point, so at last the long-smothered resentment of the lover blazed forth. He was speaking of that wondrous time when he, as the thistle, Zeherit, had so dearly loved the fair Hollandress, who was then the daughter of King Sekakis, and was reminding her, with all the fire of love, that the circumstance of his battle with the Leech-Prince had given him the most incontestable right to her hand. On her part, she declared that she well remembered it, and had already felt the foreboding of it, when Pepusch gazed on her with the thistle-glance; she spoke, too, so sweetly of these wonderful matters, seemed so inspired with love to the thistle, Zeherit, who had been destined to study at Jena, and then again find the Princess Gamaheh in Berlin, that George Pepusch fancied himself in the El Dorado of all delight. The lovers stood at the window, and the little one suffered her enamoured friend to wind his arm about her. In this familiar position they caressed each other, for to that at last came the dreamy talk about the wonders in Famagusta, when it chanced that a handsome officer of the guards passed by in a brand-new uniform, and familiarly greeted the little one, whom he knew from the evening entertainments; Dörtje had half closed her eyes and turned away her head from the street, so that one would have thought it was impossible for her to see the officer, but great is the magic of a fine new uniform! The little one﻿—roused, perhaps, by the clatter of the sabre on the pavement﻿—opened her eyes broad and bright, twisted herself from George’s arm, flung open the window, threw a kiss to the officer, and watched him till he had disappeared round the corner.

===Chinese===
“老天爷！您怎么哑了，先生！”小姑娘说着，用一只想象得出最可爱的手指，轻轻点了点乔治的胸口；一道电火花顺着那指尖射进他的心，他从迷乱中醒了过来。他如痴如狂地抓住她的手，在上面盖满火烫的吻，喊道：“天上的、天使般的人儿！”如此等等，如此等等，如此等等。好心的读者不难想象，乔治·佩普施在这样一个时刻还会喊出些什么来。只需说一句就够：她把他的爱情宣言，收得再可心不过；在列文虎克大厅角落里的这个决定命运的瞬间，结出了一段恋情——它先是把好心的乔治·佩普施捧上天堂，随后又把他掷进地狱。他天生一副忧郁的性子，加上急躁多疑，多尔特耶的举止便免不了惹出许多小小的醋意来。而恰恰是这些醋意，搔着了多尔特耶那促狭的心性；变着法儿折磨可怜的乔治·佩普施，正是她的乐趣。可凡事总得有个限度，于是情人心中郁积已久的怨愤，终于爆发了。他正说到那个奇妙的年代——他身为蓟草精泽赫里特，怎样深爱着这位当时还是塞卡基斯王之女的美丽的荷兰姑娘；他带着全部爱的火焰提醒她说，他同水蛭王子的一仗，给了他对她玉手最无可辩驳的权利。她呢，声称这一切她记得清清楚楚，早在佩普施用蓟草精的目光凝望她时，她便有了预感；她还把这些奇妙的事情讲得那样甜蜜，对那株蓟草精泽赫里特显得那样一往情深——这株蓟草命定要先到耶拿去求学，而后又在柏林找回加玛赫公主——叫乔治·佩普施只觉得，自己置身在人间一切欢愉的黄金国（El Dorado）里了。恋人俩站在窗前，小姑娘听任她那位痴心的朋友把胳膊绕上自己。就以这样亲昵的姿势，两人你怜我爱——因为那场关于法马古斯塔种种奇事的梦呓，到头来正落到这份亲昵上——恰巧有一位英俊的卫队军官，穿着一身崭新的制服从街上走过，亲亲热热地招呼这位他从晚间聚会上认识的小姑娘。多尔特耶原本半阖着眼，把头从街那边扭开，照理说决计看不见那军官才是；可一身漂亮的新制服，魔力何其大啊！小姑娘——多半是叫军刀敲在石板路上的声响惊动的吧——把眼睛睁得又大又亮，从乔治的臂弯里一扭脱身，猛地推开窗子，朝那军官飞去一个吻，还一直望到他拐过街角不见了踪影。

===Original===
“Gamaheh!” shouted George Pepusch, quite beside himself, “Gamaheh! What is this? Do you mock me? Is this the faith you have promised to your Thistle?”

The little one turned round upon her heel, burst into a loud laughter, and exclaimed﻿—

“Go, go, George; if I am the daughter of the worthy old King Sekakis, if you are the thistle, Zeherit, that dear officer is the genius, Thetel, who, in fact, pleases me much better than the sad thorny thistle.”

With this she darted away through the door, while George Pepusch, as might be expected, fell immediately into a fit of desperation, and rushed down the steps as if he had been driven by a thousand devils. Fate would have it, that he met a friend, in a post-chaise, who was leaving Berlin, upon which he called out, “Halt! I go with you,” flew home, donned a great coat, put money in his purse, gave the key of his room to the hostess, seated himself in the chaise, and posted off with his friend.

===Chinese===
“加玛赫！”乔治·佩普施忘乎所以地大喊，“加玛赫！这是怎么回事？你在戏弄我吗？这就是你许给你那蓟草精的信义吗？”

小姑娘脚跟一旋，放声大笑，喊道——

“去吧，去吧，乔治；就算我是那位可敬的老塞卡基斯王的女儿，就算你是蓟草精泽赫里特，那位可爱的军官可是精灵忒特尔——说实在的，他比你这株愁眉苦脸、浑身带刺的蓟草，可讨我喜欢多了。”

说罢，她一阵风似的穿门而去；乔治·佩普施呢，不出所料，登时陷进一阵绝望，冲下台阶，仿佛有一千个魔鬼在后头撵着。命运偏偏安排，他遇上一位朋友，正坐着邮车离开柏林；他朝车上喊：“停一停！我跟你走！”随即飞奔回家，套上一件大衣，往钱袋里塞了钱，把房间钥匙交给女房东，坐进邮车，同朋友一道登程去了。

===Original===
Notwithstanding this hostile separation, his love to the fair Hollandress was by no means extinguished﻿—and just as little could he resolve to give up the fair claims, which, as the thistle Zeherit, he thought he had to the hand and heart of Gamaheh﻿—he renewed therefore his pretensions, when some years afterwards he met with Leeuwenhoek again at the Hague, and how zealously he followed her in Frankfurt the reader has learned already.

George Pepusch was wandering through the streets at night, quite inconsolable, when his attention was attracted by an unusually bright light that fell upon the street from a crevice in the window-shutter in the lower room of a large house. He thought that there must be fire in the chamber, and swung himself up by means of the ironwork to look in. Boundless was his surprise at what he saw. A large fire blazed in the chimney, which was opposite to the window, before which sat, or rather lay, the little Hollandress in a broad old-fashioned armchair, dressed out like an angel. She seemed to sleep, while a withered old man knelt before the fire, and with spectacles on his nose, peeped into a kettle, in which he was probably brewing some potion. Pepusch was trying to raise himself higher to get a better view of the group, when he felt himself seized by the legs, and violently pulled down. A harsh voice exclaimed, “Now only see the rascal! To the watchhouse, my master!” It was the watchman who had observed George climbing up the window, and could not suppose otherwise than that he wanted to break into the house. In spite of all protestations, George Pepusch was dragged off by the watchman, to whose help the patrol had hastened, and thus his nightly wandering ended merrily in the watchhouse.

===Chinese===
尽管这样不欢而散，他对那位美丽荷兰姑娘的爱却绝没有熄灭——同样，他也下不了决心放弃那些体面的权利要求：他自以为，凭着蓟草精泽赫里特的身份，他对加玛赫的手和心享有这些权利——因此，几年之后，他在海牙同列文虎克重逢，便又重申旧请；至于他在法兰克福怎样一片痴心地追随着她，读者已经知道了。

乔治·佩普施夜里在街上踯躅，愁肠百结。忽然，一束异样明亮的灯光，从一座大宅底层房间的百叶窗缝里投到街上，把他的目光引住了。他想屋里必定失了火，便攀着窗上的铁器荡上去张望。眼前的景象令他惊讶万分：烟囱里——它正对着窗子——燃着一炉旺火；火前坐着的，不如说躺着的，是那位小小的荷兰美人，歪在一把宽大的老式扶手椅里，打扮得像个天使。她似乎睡着了；一个干瘪的老头儿跪在炉火前，鼻梁上架着眼镜，正朝一口小锅里张望，锅里多半熬着什么药剂。佩普施正想再往上攀，好看清楚这一景，忽觉双腿被人抓住，狠狠拽了下来。一个刺耳的嗓子喊道：“快瞧瞧这无赖！送岗亭去，我的少爷！”那是一个更夫，方才瞧见乔治爬窗，只当他是要撬门进宅。任凭乔治·佩普施百般分辩，还是被更夫拖走了——巡逻队也赶来帮忙——他这一夜的漫游，就这样在岗亭里快快活活地收了场。

## III Third Adventure / 三 第三奇遇

===Original===
He who has experienced such things in one evening as Mr. Peregrine Tyss, and who is consequently in such a state of mind, cannot possibly sleep well. He rolled about restless on his bed, and, when he fell into that sort of delirium which usually precedes sleep, he again held the little creature in his arms, and felt warm glowing kisses on his lips. Then he would start up and fancy, even when awake, that he heard the sweet voice of Alina. He would burn with desire that she might not have fled, and yet again would fear that she might return and snare him in a net, from which he could not extricate himself. This war of contrary feelings straightened his breast, and filled it at the same time with a sweet pain, such as he had never felt before.

“Sleep not, Peregrine; sleep not, generous man: I must speak with you directly,” was lisped close by Peregrine, and still the voice went on with “sleep not, sleep not,” till at last he opened his eyes, which he had closed only to see Alina more distinctly. By the light of the lamp he perceived a little monster, scarce a span long, that sat upon the white counterpane, and which at first terrified him, but in the next moment he grasped boldly at it with his hand, to convince himself whether he was or was not deceived by his fancy, but the little monster had immediately disappeared without leaving a trace behind.

Though it was not requisite to give a minute description of the fair Alina, Dörtje Elverdink, or Princess Gamaheh﻿—for the reader has long ago known that these were one and the same person apparently split into three﻿—it is, on the contrary, quite requisite to narrowly portray the little monster that sat upon the counterpane, and caused so much terror to Mr. Peregrine Tyss.

===Chinese===
谁要是在一个晚上像佩雷格林·蒂斯（Peregrine Tyss）先生那样经历了这许多事情、落进这般心绪之中，就不可能睡得安稳。他在床上翻来覆去，焦躁不宁；待到坠入那种往往先于沉睡而来的迷离恍惚之境，他又把那小小的人儿搂在怀里，唇上觉出温热灼烫的亲吻。他惊跳起来，哪怕醒着，也恍恍惚惚听见阿莉娜（Alina）那甜美的嗓音。他炽烈地盼着她不曾逃走，转而又害怕她当真回来，把他诱进一张他再也挣脱不得的罗网。这彼此交战的感情绷紧了他的胸膛，同时又让胸中盛满一种他从未尝过的、甜蜜的痛楚。

"别睡，佩雷格林；别睡，慷慨的人：我必须立刻同你说话。"这声音就在佩雷格林耳边细声念叨，还一个劲儿念着"别睡，别睡"，直到他终于睁开眼睛——他方才闭眼，原是为了把阿莉娜看得更真切些。借着灯光，他看见一个小小的怪物端坐在白色床罩上，身长还不足一拃（张开手掌，拇指到小指的距离）。起初他吓了一跳，下一刻却大胆地伸手去抓，想弄清自己究竟是不是被幻象骗了；可那小怪物早已消失得无影无踪，半点痕迹也不曾留下。

美丽的阿莉娜、多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克（Dörtje Elverdink）或者加玛赫公主（Princess Gamaheh）究竟是什么模样，本不必再细细描摹——因为读者早就知道，这三位分明是同一个人，只是化作了三身；反过来，那个端坐床罩之上、把佩雷格林·蒂斯先生吓得魂不附体的小怪物，倒是很有必要细细刻画一番的。

===Original===
As already mentioned, the creature was scarcely a span long. In his bird-shaped head gleamed a pair of round sparkling eyes, and from his sparrow beak protruded a long sharp thing like a rapier, while two horns came out from the forehead close below the beak. The neck began close under the head also, in the manner of a bird, but grew thicker and thicker, so that without any interruption the former grew to a shapeless body, almost like a hazelnut, and seemed covered with dark-brown scales like the armadillo. But the strangest part was the formation of the arms and legs; the two former had joints, and were rooted in the creature’s cheeks, close by the beak; immediately under these arms was a pair of legs, and still farther on another pair, both double-jointed like the arms. These last feet appeared to be those on which the creature really relied, for besides that they were longer and stronger than the others, he wore upon them very handsome golden boots with diamond spurs.

The little monster having so completely vanished upon Peregrine’s attempt to seize it, he would have taken the whole for an illusion of his excited fancy, if directly afterwards a thin voice had not been audible, exclaiming:

===Chinese===
如前所述，这小东西身长不足一拃。他那鸟形的脑袋上闪着一对滚圆发亮的眼睛，麻雀似的喙中斜插出一件又长又尖、形如细剑的东西，喙根下面的额头上则支棱出两只角。脖子也像鸟儿那样紧贴着脑袋生出，却越往下越粗，毫无间隔地长成一个不成形状的身子，几乎像一颗榛子，周身仿佛覆着犰狳一般的深褐色鳞甲。最古怪的还是臂腿的构造：前面一对手臂生有关节，扎根在小东西的面颊上，紧挨着喙；这对手臂底下紧挨着又是一双腿，再往后还有一对，都同手臂一样是双重关节。末后这一双脚，想来才是小东西真正倚仗的，因为它们不仅比其余的更长更壮，上面还穿着非常气派的金色靴子，靴上缀着钻石马刺。

佩雷格林伸手一抓，小怪物竟消失得这般干净利落；若不是紧接着便听得见一个细细的声音高声说话，他准会把这一幕整个当作自己亢奋的想象生出的幻象。那声音喊道：

===Original===
“Good heavens! Mr. Peregine Tyss! have I really been mistaken in you? Yesterday you acted so nobly towards me, and now that I want to show my gratitude, you grasp at me with a murderous hand! But perhaps my form displeased you, and I did wrong in showing myself to you microscopically, that you might be sure to see me, which, as you may well suppose, is no such easy matter; in fact, I am still sitting upon your white counterpane, and yet you cannot perceive me. Don’t take it amiss, Peregrine, but, in truth, your optical nerves are a little too gross for my thin form. Only promise me, however, that I shall be safe with you, and that you will not make any hostile attempts upon me, and I will come close to you and tell you many things, which it would be as well that you knew now.”

===Chinese===
"天哪！佩雷格林·蒂斯先生！我当真看错你了不成？昨天你待我那般高义，如今我正想表示感激，你却伸出凶残的手来抓我！不过，也许是我不讨人喜欢的模样冒犯了你，我不该以显微之身向你显形——为的是保你一眼便能看到我，这件事你尽可以想见，绝非易事；事实上，我此刻仍旧坐在你的白床罩上，你却看不见我。别见怪，佩雷格林，说实在的，你的视觉神经对我这纤细的身形而言，实在粗钝了些。只消你答应我，我在你这里万无一失，你不会再对我下任何敌手的狠招，我就挨到你跟前，告诉你许多事情——这些事，你眼下知道了才好。"

===Original===
“In the first place,” replied Mr. Tyss to the voice, “tell me, my good unknown friend, who you are; the rest will easily follow of itself. In the meantime I can assure you beforehand, that anything hostile is not at all in my disposition, and that I will continue to act nobly towards you, though at present I cannot comprehend in what way I have evinced my nobleness. Keep, however, your incognito, for your appearance is not the most agreeable.”

The voice, after a little hemming and coughing, continued, “You are, I repeat it with pleasure, a noble man, Mr. Peregrine, but not particularly deep in science, and above all, a little inexperienced, or you would have recognised me at the first glance. I might boast a little and say that I am one of the mightiest of kings, and rule over many, many millions, but from a natural modesty, and because, after all, the expression ‘king’ is not exactly correct, I will pass it over. Amongst the people, at whose head I have the honour to be, a republican constitution prevails. A senate, which at most can consist of forty-five thousand, nine hundred and ninety-nine members, for the greater facility of voting, holds the place of regent, and he who presides over this senate has the name of master, because, in all the affairs of life, he must really be a master. Without farther circumlocution, I will now confess to you that I, who now speak to you without your seeing me, am none other than this Master Flea. That you know my people I do not make the least doubt, for most assuredly, worthy sir, you have already nourished many of them with your own blood. Hence you must needs be aware that they are animated by an untameable love of freedom, and indeed are a set of springalds, who are inclined to keep off anything like solidity of form by a continual leaping and skipping. You will easily perceive what talents must be requisite to govern such a people, and will therefore feel for me a becoming respect. Assure me of that, Mr. Peregrine, before I proceed any farther.”

===Chinese===
"首先，"蒂斯先生对着那声音答道，"告诉我，好心的无名朋友，你究竟是谁；其余的，自然迎刃而解。这会儿我不妨先向你担保：敌意之类，全然不合我的天性；我也愿意继续以高义待你，尽管眼下我还想不明白，我这份高义究竟从何谈起。不过，你还是保持你的匿名吧，因为你的尊容实在说不上讨人喜欢。"

那声音轻轻清了清嗓子、咳了两声，继续说道："我很乐意再说一遍，佩雷格林先生，你是个高尚的人，只是学问不怎么深，尤其是见识还欠些火候，不然你一眼就该认出我来了。我本可以稍稍自夸一番，说自己是最威严的君王之一，统治着千千万万的子民；但出于天生的谦逊，也因为「王」这个字眼终究算不得确切，我还是略过不提吧。在我有幸统领的这个民族里，奉行的是共和政体。一个元老院——为了表决便利，至多可由四万五千九百九十九名元老组成——代行摄政之职；主持这个元老院的人，名号唤作「大师」，因为在人生的一切事务上，他都必须真正称得上一位大师。不必再绕弯子了，我这就向你招认：这个此刻对你说话、你却看不见的，不是别人，正是跳蚤大师（Master Flea）本人。你认得我的子民，这一点我毫不怀疑，因为可以断言，可敬的先生，你早已用自己的血养肥过他们当中的许多个。由此你也必定知道，我的子民心怀一种驯服不了的自由之爱，实在是一帮轻狂跳脱的野小子，一心想靠没完没了的蹦蹦跳跳，把一切沉稳厚重之类的品性统统拒之门外。你很容易想见，要治理这样一个民族需要何等的才干；因此，你对我自会生出几分应有的敬意。先把这一点向我担保了，佩雷格林先生，我才好继续往下讲。"

===Original===
For some moments it seemed to Mr. Tyss as if a great mill wheel were turning round in his head, but he soon became more composed, and began to think that the appearance of the strange lady at the bookbinder’s was just as wonderful as the present one, which was perhaps, after all, nothing more than a natural continuation of the singular history in which he had become involved. He therefore declared to Master Flea that he respected him prodigiously for his uncommon talents, and was the more anxious to know him better, as his voice sounded very sweetly, and there was a certain delicacy in his speech which betrayed a delicate form of body, whereat Master Flea continued:

===Chinese===
有那么片刻，蒂斯先生只觉得脑袋里像有一只巨大的水车轮子轰隆隆打转；但他很快便镇定下来，转念想道：装订匠铺子里那位陌生小姐的现身，本就同眼前这桩一样离奇，而眼前这桩，说不定不过是那段他身不由己卷入的奇特经历的自然延续罢了。于是他向跳蚤大师声明：以其过人的才干，他对他敬服得五体投地，而且愈发想同他深交，因为他的嗓音听来十分甜美，谈吐间自有一种纤雅，泄露了他身形的纤秀。听了这话，跳蚤大师继续说道：
===Original===
“I thank you much, my best Mr. Tyss, for your favourable opinion, and hope soon to convince you that you are not mistaken in me. In the meantime, that you may learn what service you have rendered me, it is requisite that I should impart to you my whole history. Know then that my father was the renowned﻿ ﻿… (Yet stay; it just occurs to me that the beautiful gift of patience has become remarkably rare of late amongst readers and auditors, and that copious memoirs, once so much admired, are now detestable: I will therefore touch lightly and episodically that part only which is more immediately connected with my abode with you.)﻿ ﻿… In knowing that I am really Master Flea, you must know me for a man of the most extensive learning, of the most profound experience in all branches of knowledge. But hold! You cannot measure the degree of my information by your scale, since you are ignorant of the wonderful world in which I and my people live. How would you feel astonished if your mind could be opened to that world! It would seem to you a realm of the strangest and most incomprehensible wonders, and hence you must not feel surprised, if all which originates from that world should seem to you like a confused fairy tale, invented by an idle brain. Do not, therefore, allow yourself to be confounded, but trust my words. See: in many things my people are far superior to you men, for example: in all that regards the penetrating into the mysteries of nature, in strength, dexterity﻿—spiritual and corporeal dexterity. But we, too, have our passions, and with us, as with you, these are often the sources of great disquietudes, sometimes even of total destruction. Loved, nay adored, as I was, by my people, my mastery might have placed me upon the pinnacle of happiness, had I not been blinded by an unfortunate passion for a person who completely governed me, though she never could be my wife. But our race is in general reproached with a passion for the fair sex, that oversteps the bounds of decorum. Supposing however this reproach to be true, yet on the other hand, everyone knows. But hold: without more circumlocution, I saw the daughter of King Sekakis, the beautiful Gamaheh, and on the instant became so desperately enamoured of her, that I forgot my people﻿—myself﻿—and lived only in the delight of skipping about the fairest neck, the fairest bosom, and tickling the beauty with kisses. She often caught at me with her rosy fingers, without ever being able to seize me, and this I took for the toying of affection. But how silly is anyone in love, even when that one is Master Flea. Suffice it to say, that the odious Leech-Prince fell upon the poor Gamaheh, whom he kissed to death, but still I should have succeeded in saving my beloved, if a silly boaster and an awkward idiot had not interfered without being asked, and spoilt all. The boaster was the Thistle, Zeherit, and the idiot was the Genius, Thetel. When, however, the Genius rose in the air with the sleeping princess, I clung fast to the lace about her bosom, and thus was Gamaheh’s faithful fellow-traveller, without being perceived by him. It happened that we flew over two magi, who were observing the stars from a lofty tower. One of them directed his glass so sharply at me, that I was almost blinded by the shine of the magic instrument. A violent giddiness seized me; in vain I sought to hold fast. I tumbled down helplessly from the monstrous height, fell plump upon the nose of one of the magi, and only my lightness, my extraordinary activity, could have saved me.

===Chinese===
"多谢你了，我最亲爱的蒂斯先生，多谢你的错爱，我希望很快便能使你确信：你没有看错我。眼下，要让你明白你帮了我多大的忙，我就得把我的全部经历讲给你听。那么请听：我的父亲是大名鼎鼎的……（且住！我忽然想起，近来读者和听众们那份可贵的耐性禀赋已稀罕得很，而从前备受推崇的皇皇长篇回忆录，如今也教人齿冷：因此，凡与我在你处安身的关系不甚密切的部分，我只浮光掠影、权作插曲地一提。）……你既然知道了我是货真价实的跳蚤大师，就必得把我当作一个学识最为渊博、于一切门类学问皆有最为深刻体验的人。可是且慢！你无法拿你那把尺子来度量我学识的深浅，因为你根本不知道我和我的子民所生活的那个奇妙世界。倘若你的心智能向那个世界敞开，你该会惊成什么样啊！在你眼里，那将是一片最离奇、最不可理解的奇境；因此，凡自那个世界流传出来的东西，在你听来都像某个游手好闲的脑袋胡编出来的、颠三倒四的童话，你也就大可不必诧异了。所以，别让自己犯迷糊，只管相信我的话。你瞧：在许多方面，我的子民远胜你们人类，比如：凡是涉及洞穿自然奥秘之处，还有气力，还有灵巧——心灵的与身体的灵巧。但是我们也有我们的七情六欲，而且在我们中间，正如在你们中间一样，这些情欲常常是巨大不安的根源，有时甚至是彻底毁灭的根源。我的子民爱戴我，不，是崇拜我；若不是被一场不幸的情欲迷住了心窍——迷上一个完完全全支配了我、却永远做不成我妻子的人——凭我的大师之位，我本可以稳稳登上幸福的绝顶。我们的种族，总的说来，一向担着贪恋女色、逾越体面的名声。不过就算这指摘不假，可从另一面说，谁都知道……且住：不必再绕弯子了——我见到了塞卡基斯王（King Sekakis）的女儿、美丽的加玛赫，顷刻之间便狂热地恋上了她，恋得忘掉了我的子民——忘掉了我自己——光顾着在那最美的颈项上、最美的胸脯上蹦来跳去，用亲吻把美人儿逗得痒痒的，独自活在快活里。她常常伸出玫瑰色的手指来捉我，却从来不曾捉住过，而我竟把这当成了爱侣间的嬉闹。可是恋爱中的人有多蠢啊，哪怕这人是跳蚤大师。长话短说：那可憎的水蛭王子（the Leech-Prince）扑向了可怜的加玛赫，要把她亲吻至死；倘若没有一个蠢透的牛皮大王和一个笨手笨脚的蠢货没人相请便横插一手、搅坏了一切，我本来还是能救下我爱人的。那牛皮大王是蓟草精泽赫里特（the Thistle Zeherit），那蠢货是精灵忒特尔（the Genius Thetel）。然而，当那精灵带着昏睡的公主腾空而起时，我死死攀住她胸前的花边，就这样成了加玛赫忠实的同行旅伴，他丝毫没有察觉。恰巧我们飞过两位术士的上空，他们正在一座高塔上观星。其中一位把镜筒直直地对准了我，那法器的光亮险些把我晃瞎。一阵剧烈的眩晕向我袭来；我拼命想抓牢，却是徒劳。我从那骇人的高处无助地栽落下去，扑通一声，正正跌在其中一位术士的鼻子上，全亏我身子轻、身手又超凡地矫捷，才捡回了性命。"
===Original===
“I was still too much stunned to skip off his nose and place myself in perfect safety, when the treacherous Leeuwenhoek﻿—he was the magician﻿—caught me dexterously with his fingers, and placed me in his microscope. Notwithstanding it was night, and he was obliged to use a lamp, he was by far too practiced an observer, and too great an adept, not immediately to recognise in me the Master Flea. Delighted that a lucky chance had delivered into his hands such an important prisoner, and resolved to draw every possible advantage from it, he flung poor me into chains, and thus began a painful imprisonment, from which I was yesterday freed by you. The possession of me gave the abominable Leeuwenhoek full power over my vassals, whom he soon collected in swarms about him, and with barbarian cruelty introduced amongst us that which is called education, and which soon robbed us of all freedom, of all enjoyment of life. In regard to scholastic studies, and the arts and sciences in general, Leeuwenhoek soon discovered, to his surprise and vexation, that we knew more than himself; the higher cultivation which he forced upon us consisted chiefly in this: that we were to be something, or at least represent something. But it was precisely this being something, this representing something, that brought with it a multitude of wants which we had never known before, and which were now to be satisfied with the sweat of our brow. The barbarous Leeuwenhoek converted us into statesmen, soldiers, professors, and I know not what besides. All were obliged to wear the dress of their respective ranks, and thus arose amongst us tailors, shoemakers, hairdressers, blacksmiths, cutlers, and a multitude of other trades, only to satisfy an useless and destructive luxury. The worst of it was that Leeuwenhoek had nothing else in view than his own advantage in showing us cultivated people to men, and receiving money for it. Moreover our cultivation was set down entirely to his account, and he got the praise which belonged to us alone. Leeuwenhoek well knew that in losing me he would also lose the dominion over my people; the more closely therefore he drew the spell which bound me to him, and so much the harder was my imprisonment. I thought with ardent desire on the beautiful Gamaheh, and pondered on the means of getting tidings of her fate, but what the acutest reason could not effect, the chance of the moment itself brought about. The friend and associate of my magician, the old Swammerdam, had found the princess in the petal of a tulip, and this discovery he imparted to his friend. By means which, my good Peregrine, I forbear detailing to you, as you do not understand much about these matters, he succeeded in restoring Gamaheh to her natural shape, and bringing her back to life. In the end, however, these very wise persons proved as awkward idiots as the Genius, Thetel, and the Thistle, Zeherit. In their eagerness they had forgotten the most material point, and thus it happened that in the very same moment the princess awoke to life, she was sinking back again into death. I alone knew the cause; love to the fair one, which now flamed in my breast stronger than ever, gave me a giant’s strength: I burst my chains, sprang with one mighty bound upon her shoulder﻿—a single bite sufficed to set the freezing blood in motion: she lived. But I must tell you, Mr. Peregrine Tyss, that this bite must be repeated if the princess is to continue blooming in youth and beauty; otherwise she will dwindle away in a few months to a shrivelled little old woman. On this account, as you must see, I am quite indispensable to her, and it is only by the fear of losing me, that I can account for the black ingratitude with which she repaid my love. Without more ado she delivered me up to my tormentor, who flung me into heavier chains than ever, but to his own destruction. In spite of all the vigilance of Leeuwenhoek and Gamaheh, I at last succeeded, in an unguarded hour, in escaping from my prison. Although the heavy boots, which I had no time to pull off, hindered me considerably in my flight, yet I got safely to the shop of the toyman, of whom you bought your ware; but it was not long before, to my infinite terror, Gamaheh entered the shop. I held myself lost; you alone could save me. I gently whispered to you my distress, and you were good enough to open a little box for me, into which I quickly sprang, and in which you as quickly carried me off with you. Gamaheh sought in vain for me, and it was not till much later that she learned how and whither I had fled.

===Chinese===
"我还晕头转向，来不及从他鼻子上蹦开、躲进稳妥的地方去，那奸诈的列文虎克（Leeuwenhoek）——他正是那术士——已手疾眼快地用手指将我拿住，放进了他的显微镜。虽是夜里，不得不点灯，他到底是太老练的观察家、太高明的行家，一眼便认出我是跳蚤大师。天赐良机，把这样一个要犯送进他手里，他喜出望外，打定主意要从中榨取一切可能的好处，便把我这可怜虫扔进锁链，于是一段痛楚的囚禁开始了——直到昨天，方由你解救出来。占有了我，可恶的列文虎克便攥住了辖制我全部臣民的权柄；他很快把他们一群群收拢到身边，又以蛮族的残忍，在我们中间推行起那唤作教化的东西，而这教化不久便夺走了我们全部的自由、全部的生活乐趣。至于学业功课和一般的文艺科学，列文虎克很快又惊又恼地发现，我们懂得竟比他还多；于是他强加给我们的高等教化，主要内容便只剩下一条：我们都得成为个人物，至少也得装成个人物。可恰恰是这个成为人物、装成人物，带来了我们从前闻所未闻的一大堆需求，如今都得我们额上冒汗去挣。那野蛮的列文虎克把我们改造成政治家、军人、教授，还有天知道些什么。人人都得穿与各自身份相称的衣裳，于是我们中间冒出了裁缝、鞋匠、理发匠、铁匠、刀具匠，以及许许多多别的行当，全只为供养一种毫无用处、败家亡身的奢华。最糟的是，列文虎克把受过教化的我们陈列给人类看、从中收钱，除此之外他别无所图。再说，我们修来的教化全记在了他一人的账上，本该归于我们独有的赞誉也全让他捞了去。列文虎克深知，一旦失掉我，他对我子民的统治也会随之失掉；于是他把缚我于他身边的那道符咒收得一天紧似一天，我的囚禁也就愈发难熬。我怀着炽热的思念想着美丽的加玛赫，苦苦琢磨打听她下落的门路；可是最锐利的理智办不成的事，当日的机缘自己办成了。我那位术士的老友兼同行、老斯瓦默丹（Swammerdam）在一瓣郁金香里找到了公主，并把这个发现告诉了他的朋友。凭着某些手段——好心的佩雷格林，恕我不向你细说，因为这些事你懂得不多——他成功地把加玛赫恢复了原形，让她起死回生。然而到头来，这两位绝顶聪明的人物竟也证明自己同精灵忒特尔和蓟草精泽赫里特一样，是笨手笨脚的蠢货。他们急切之间忘掉了最要紧的一点，于是事情竟成了这样：公主在同一瞬间苏醒过来，又沉回落死亡。唯有我知道症结所在；对美人的爱此刻在我胸中烧得比以往任何时候都旺，给了我巨人般的膂力：我挣断锁链，奋力一跃，跳上她的肩头——只消一口，便让冻僵的血液重新流转起来：她活了。可是我必须告诉你，佩雷格林·蒂斯先生，公主要想青春常驻、容颜不老，这一口便必得反复施为；否则不出几个月，她便会干缩成一个小小的皱皮老妪。因此你也看得出来，我对她是万万缺不得的；而她竟拿漆黑的忘恩负义来报答我的爱，我也只能拿生怕失掉我来解释。她不由分说，把我交到那折磨我的人手里；他把我在比先前更沉重的镣铐里锁得更紧——这却正给他自己掘下了坟墓。尽管列文虎克和加玛赫百般提防，我终究瞅准一个疏于看守的时辰，逃出了牢笼。那双沉重的靴子——我来不及脱掉——大大拖累了我奔逃的脚步，可我还是平安赶到了那家玩具铺，就是你买玩具的那一家；然而没过多久，加玛赫竟也进了铺子，吓得我魂飞天外。我自料必死无疑；能救我的只有你。我悄悄向你耳语我的危难，你也真好心，替我打开一只小盒子，我一跃而入，你也随即把我连盒子一并带走了。加玛赫寻我不着，直到很久以后，才知道我是怎样逃走、又逃到了哪里。"
===Original===
“As soon as I was free, Leeuwenhoek lost all power over my people, who immediately slipped away, and in mockery left the tyrant peppercorns, fruit-stones, and suchlike, in their clothes. Again then my hearty thanks, kind, noble Mr. Peregrine, for the great benefit you have done me, and which I know as well as anyone how to estimate. Permit me, as a free man, to remain a little time with you; I can be useful to you in many important affairs of your life beyond what you may expect. To be sure there might be danger if you should become enamoured of the fair one﻿—”

“What do you say?” interrupted Peregrine, “What do you say, Master? I﻿—I enamoured!”

“Even so,” continued Master Flea, “Think of my terror, of my anxiety, when you entered yesterday with the princess in your arms, glowing with passion, and she employing every seductive art﻿—as she well knows how﻿—to persuade you to surrender me. Ah, then I perceived your nobleness in its full extent, when you remained immoveable, dexterously feigning as if you knew nothing of my being with you, as if you did not even understand what the princess wanted.”

===Chinese===
"我一脱身，列文虎克对我子民的权柄便登时烟消云散；他们当即四散溜走，只在衣服里给那暴君留下些胡椒粒、果核之类，聊作嘲弄。那么，仁厚高义的佩雷格林先生，请再受我衷心一谢：你帮了我这样的大忙，这份恩情的分量，我同任何人一样掂量得出。请容许我以自由之身，在你这里盘桓些时日；在你一生许多要紧的事体上，我能出乎你意料地帮上大忙。不过，倘若你恋上了那位美人，那倒当真可能有危险——"

"你说什么？"佩雷格林打断了他，"你说什么，大师？我——我恋上了！"

"正是如此。"跳蚤大师接着说，"想想我昨天的惊骇与焦灼吧：你抱着公主走进来，欲火中烧；她呢，正使出浑身解数的媚术——这一套她最是拿手——劝你把我交出去。啊，正是在那一刻，我把你的高义看了个通透：你岿然不动，机敏地装作全然不知我就在你身边，甚至装作压根听不懂公主要的是什么。"

===Original===
“And that was precisely the truth of the matter,” said Peregrine, interrupting Master Flea anew. “You are attributing things as a merit to me, of which I had not the slightest suspicion. In the shop where I bought the toys, I neither saw you nor the fair damsel, who sought me at the bookbinder’s, and whom you are strangely pleased to call the Princess Gamaheh. It was quite unknown to me, that amongst the boxes, where I expected to find leaden soldiers, there was an empty one in which you were lurking, and how could I possibly guess that you were the prisoner whom the pretty child was requiring with such impetuosity? Don’t be whimsical, Master Flea, and dream of things, of which I had not the slightest conception.”

“Ah,” replied Master Flea, “you would dexterously avoid my thanks, kind Mr. Peregrine, and this gives me, to my great consolation, a farther lively proof of your noble way of thinking. Learn, generous man, that all the efforts of Leeuwenhoek and Gamaheh to regain me are fruitless, so long as you afford me your protection. You must voluntarily give me up to my tormentors; all other means are to no purpose﻿—Mr. Peregine Tyss, you are in love!”

“Do not talk so!” exclaimed Peregrine. “Do not call by the name of love a foolish momentary ebullition, which is already past.”

===Chinese===
"可那恰恰是实情啊。"佩雷格林再次打断了跳蚤大师。"你把这些算作我的功德，我却连一点影子都不曾察觉。在我买玩具的那家铺子里，我既没看见你，也没看见那位到装订匠铺子里寻过我的美丽少女——你却古里古怪地偏要称她加玛赫公主。我全然不知，在那些我指望找到铅制小兵的盒子中间，竟有一只空盒里潜伏着你；我又怎么可能猜到，你就是那漂亮孩子气势汹汹索要的囚徒呢？别异想天开了，跳蚤大师，别去梦见那些我连丝毫概念都没有的事情。"

"啊，"跳蚤大师答道，"仁厚的佩雷格林先生，你想机灵地躲开我的道谢，这倒给了我莫大的安慰，进一步活生生地证明了你心地的高贵。听着，慷慨的人：只要你肯庇护我，列文虎克和加玛赫想夺回我的一切努力，都是徒劳。要把我交到折磨我的人手里，必须出于你的自愿；其余的法子一概无用——佩雷格林·蒂斯先生，你恋爱了！"

"别这么说！"佩雷格林喊道。"别把一阵愚蠢的、转瞬即逝的心头沸热，唤作爱情。"

===Original===
Peregrine felt the colour rushing up into his cheeks and forehead, and giving him the lie. He crept under the bedclothes. Master Flea continued:

“It is not to be wondered at if you were unable to resist the surprising charms of the princess, especially as she employed many dangerous arts to captivate you. Nor is the storm yet over. The malicious little thing will put in practice many a trick to catch you in her love-toils, as, indeed, every woman can, without exactly being a Princess Gamaheh. She will try to get you so completely in her power, that you shall only live for her and her wishes, and then﻿—woe to me! It will come to this question: is your nobleness strong enough to conquer your passion, or will you prefer yielding to Gamaheh’s wishes, and thus replunge into misery not only your little protégé, but the whole people whom you have released from a wretched slavery? Or, again, will you resist the allurements of a treacherous creature, and thus confirm my happiness and that of my subjects? Oh that you would promise me the last! That you *could*!”

“Master,” replied Peregrine, drawing the bedclothes away from his face, “Dear Master, you are right: nothing is more dangerous than the temptations of women. They are all false, all malicious; they play with us as cats with mice, and for our tenderest exertions we reap nothing but contempt and mockery. Hence it is that formerly a cold deathlike perspiration used to stand upon my brow as soon as any woman-creature approached me, and I myself believe that there must be something peculiar about the fair Alina, or Princess Gamaheh, as you will have it, although, with my plain human reason, I do not comprehend all that you are saying, but rather feel as if I were in some wild dream, or reading *The Thousand and One Nights*. Be all this, however, as it may, you have put yourself under my protection, dear Master, and nothing shall persuade me to deliver you up to your enemies; as to the seductive maiden, I will not see her again. This I promise solemnly, and would give my hand upon it, had you one to receive it and return the honourable pledge.”

===Chinese===
佩雷格林觉得血直往两颊和额头上涌，一齐涌出来拆穿他的谎话。他缩进了被窝。跳蚤大师继续说道：

"你抵受不住公主那惊人的魅力，这不足为怪，何况她还使出了许多凶险的手段来蛊惑你。而且，这场风暴还没有过去。那存心不良的小东西还会使出一个个圈套，把你缠进她的情网；说实话，这种本事天下的女人个个都有，倒不一定非得是位加玛赫公主。她会设法把你完完全全攥进手心，让你只为她、只为她的心愿而活；到那时——我可就惨了！问题终归要落到这一点上：你的高义够不够坚强，能不能降服你的情欲？还是你宁愿屈从加玛赫的心愿，从而不仅把你小小的被保护人、还把整个被你从悲惨奴役中解救出来的民族，重新推回苦海？又或者，你会抵住那奸诈尤物的诱惑，从而坐实我和我臣民的幸福？哦，但愿你答应我走最后这条路！但愿你「能」！"

"大师，"佩雷格林答道，一面把被窝从脸上拉开，"亲爱的大师，你说得对：再没有比女人的诱惑更危险的了。她们个个虚假，个个歹毒；她们耍弄我们，就像猫耍弄老鼠；我们掏出最柔软的殷勤，换来的却只有轻蔑和讥笑。正因为如此，从前但凡有女性生灵走近我身边，我额头上便要沁出一层死一般冰冷的汗水。我自己也相信，美丽的阿莉娜——或者照你的说法，加玛赫公主——身上必定有些特殊的来历；尽管凭我这点平平常常的人类理智，你说的这些我领会不了，倒只觉得自己像在一场狂乱的梦里，或者正在读《一千零一夜》。不过，任凭这一切如何，亲爱的大师，你既已置身于我的保护之下，那么无论什么都休想劝我把你交到你的仇敌手里；至于那迷人的少女，我不会再见她了。这一点我郑重许诺，还要同你击掌为凭——只可惜你没有手来接受它，再把这个信物郑重还我。"

===Original===
With this Peregrine stretched out his arm far upon the bedclothes.

“Now,” exclaimed the little Invisible, “Now I am quite consoled, quite at ease. If I have no hand to offer you, at least permit me to prick you in the right thumb, partly to testify my extreme satisfaction, and partly to seal our bond of friendship more assuredly.”

At the same moment Peregrine felt in the thumb of his right hand a bite, which smarted so sensibly, as to prove it could have come only from the first Master of all the fleas.

“You bite like a little devil!” cried Peregrine.

===Chinese===
说着，佩雷格林把胳膊远远地伸到了床罩上。

"好哇！"那看不见的小家伙喊道，"这下我全然宽心、全然踏实了。既然我没有手可以伸给你，那就至少容我在你的右手拇指上刺一下，一来表明我心满意足，二来把我们的友谊之盟封得更牢靠些。"

就在同一瞬间，佩雷格林感到右手拇指上被叮了一口，痛得那么分明，足见这一口只可能出自天下跳蚤的头一位大师。

"你咬起人来活像个小魔鬼！"佩雷格林叫道。
===Original===
“Take it,” replied Master Flea, “as a lively token of my honourable intentions. But it is fit that I should offer to you, as a pledge of my gratitude, a gift which belongs to the most extraordinary productions of art. It is nothing else than a microscope, made by a very dexterous optician of my people, while he was in Leeuwenhoek’s service. The instrument will appear somewhat small to you, for, in reality, it is about a hundred and twenty times smaller than a grain of sand, but its use will not allow of any peculiar greatness. It is this: I place the glass in the pupil of your left eye, and this eye immediately becomes microscopic. As I wish to surprise you with the effect of it, I will say no more about it for the present, and will only entreat that I may be permitted to perform the microscopic operation whenever I see that it will do you any important service. And now sleep well, Mr. Peregrine; you have need of rest.”

Peregrine, in reality, fell asleep, and did not awake till full morning, when he heard the well-known scratching of old Alina’s broom; she was sweeping out the next room. A little child, who was conscious of some mischief, could not tremble more at his mother’s rod than Mr. Peregrine trembled in the fear of the old woman’s reproaches. At length she came in with the coffee. Peregrine glanced at her through the bed-curtains, which he had drawn close, and was not a little surprised at the clear sunshine which overspread the old woman’s face.

===Chinese===
"就把这一口，"跳蚤大师答道，"当作我光明磊落的心意所留的鲜活印记吧。不过，作为感激的信物，我也理当送你一件礼物，它属于艺术制品中最不寻常的杰作之列。它不是别的，正是一架显微镜，出自我们民族一位极其灵巧的光学艺匠之手，那时他还在列文虎克手下当差。这件器物在你眼里会显得小了些，因为实际上，它比一粒沙子还要小上约莫一百二十倍；可论用处，它却丝毫不因个头小而折损。用法是这样：我把镜片嵌进你左眼的瞳孔，这只眼睛便立时化作显微镜眼。效果如何，我想给你一个惊喜，眼下就不多说了；只恳求你允许我每逢看出它能于你有重要用处的时候，便施行这番显微手术。好了，安睡吧，佩雷格林先生；你需要休息。"

佩雷格林果真睡着了，一觉睡到大天亮才醒，醒来时听见老阿莉娜那把扫帚熟悉的沙沙声：她正在打扫隔壁的房间。一个自知闯了祸的小孩挨在母亲笞条跟前时的哆嗦，也不过如此——佩雷格林先生一想到老太太的数落，就是这么发抖的。她终于端着咖啡进来了。佩雷格林从拉得严严实实的床帐缝里瞟了她一眼，见老太太脸上竟布满一片晴朗的阳光，不由得相当惊讶。

===Original===
“Are you still asleep, my dear Mr. Tyss?” she asked in one of the softest tones of which her voice was capable, and Peregrine, taking courage, answered just as softly,

“No, my dear Alina. Lay the breakfast upon the table; I will get up directly.”

But when he did really rise, it seemed to him as if the sweet breath of the creature, who had lain in his arms, was waving through the chamber, he felt so strangely and so anxiously. He would have given all the world to know what had become of the mystery of his passion, for like this mystery itself, the fair one had appeared and vanished.

While he was in vain endeavouring to drink his coffee and eat his toast﻿—every morsel of which was bitter in his mouth﻿—Alina entered, and busied herself about this and that, murmuring all the time to herself, “Strange! incredible! What things one sees! Who would have thought it?”

===Chinese===
"您还睡着吗，亲爱的蒂斯先生？"她用她那嗓音所能发出的最温柔的调子问道。佩雷格林壮起胆子，也照样轻声答道：

"没睡，亲爱的阿莉娜。把早点放在桌上吧；我这就起来。"

可等他真的起身，他又恍然觉得，那个曾在他怀里偎过的小人儿，那甜蜜的气息仿佛还在房间里荡漾；他心里又是异样，又是惶惶不安。他情愿舍出整个世界，只求知道他那段痴情的谜落了个什么下场；因为那位美人儿，正同这谜一样：出现了，又消散了。

他徒然地想喝咖啡、吃吐司——每一口到了嘴里都是苦的——这时阿莉娜走进来，东摸摸西弄弄地忙活，嘴里一直喃喃自语："怪事！难以置信！这种事情也叫人见着了！谁想得到呢？"

===Original===
Peregrine, whose heart beat so strongly that he could bear it no longer, asked, “What is so strange, dear Alina?”

“All manner of things! All manner of things!” replied the old woman, laughing cunningly, while she went on with her occupation of setting the rooms to rights. Peregrine’s breast was ready to burst, and he involuntarily exclaimed, in a tone of languishing pain, “Ah! Alina!”

“Yes, Mr. Tyss, here I am; what are your commands?” replied Alina, spreading herself out before Peregrine, as if in expectation of his orders.

Peregrine stared at the copper face of the old woman, and all his fears were lost in the disgust which filled him on the sudden. He asked in a tolerably harsh tone:

===Chinese===
佩雷格林的心跳得这么急，再也按捺不住，问道："什么事这么怪，亲爱的阿莉娜？"

"各式各样的事！各式各样的事！"老太太狡黠地笑着回答，手上收拾房间的活计并不停歇。佩雷格林的胸膛快要撑破了，不由自主地、用一种萎顿哀痛的声调喊出声来："唉！阿莉娜！"

"哎，蒂斯先生，我在这儿呢；您有什么吩咐？"阿莉娜答道，在佩雷格林面前挺身站定，一副恭候差遣的模样。

佩雷格林盯着老太太那张黄铜色的脸，满心的惶恐霎时间全被陡然涌起的厌恶吞没了。他用相当生硬的口气问：

===Original===
“What has become of the strange lady who was here yesterday evening? Did you open the door for her? Did you look to a coach for her, as I ordered? Was she taken home?”

“Open doors!” said the old woman with an abominable grin, which she intended for a sly laugh. “Look to a coach! taken home! There was no need of all this: the fair damsel is in the house, and won’t leave the house for the present.”

Peregrine started up in joyful alarm, and she now proceeded to tell him how, when the lady was leaping down the stairs in a way that almost stunned her, Mr. Swammer stood below, at the door of his room, with an immense branch-candlestick in his hand. The old gentleman, with a profusion of bows, contrary to his usual custom, invited the lady into his apartment, and she slipped in without any hesitation, and her host locked and bolted the door.

===Chinese===
"昨天晚上来的那位陌生小姐后来怎么样了？你给她开的门吗？照我的吩咐，替她雇马车了吗？把她送回家了吗？"

"开门！"老太太咧开嘴，做出一个难看的鬼脸——她原是想来个狡黠的微笑。"雇马车！送回家！这些都用不着：那位美丽的小姐就在这栋房子里，眼下还不打算离开呢。"

佩雷格林又惊又喜，霍地跳起身来；老太太这才接着告诉他：那位小姐下楼梯时几乎是三级并作一级飞奔而下，险些把她吓晕过去，而斯瓦默先生（Mr. Swammer）当时正站在楼下自己房门口，手里擎着一座巨大的多枝烛台。老先生一反平日的习惯，连连躬身行礼，把小姐让进了自己的寓所；她毫不迟疑地溜了进去，主人随即锁上房门，并且上了闩。
===Original===
The conduct of the misanthropic Swammer was too strange for Alina not to listen at the door, and peep a little through the keyhole. She then saw him standing in the middle of the room, and talking so wisely and pathetically to the lady, that she herself had wept, though she had not understood a single word, he having spoken in a foreign language. She could not think otherwise than that the old gentleman had laboured to bring her back to the paths of virtue, for his vehemence had gradually increased, till the damsel at last sank upon her knees and kissed his hand with great humility: she had even wept a little. Upon this he lifted her up very kindly, kissed her forehead﻿—in doing which he was forced to stoop terribly﻿—and then led her to an armchair. He next busied himself in making a fire, brought some spices, and, as far as she could perceive, began to mull some wine. Unluckily the old woman had just then taken snuff, and sneezed aloud, upon which Swammer, stretching out his arm to the door, exclaimed with a terrible voice, that went through the marrow of her bones, “Away with thee, listening Satan!” She knew not how she had got off and into her bed, but in the morning, upon opening her eyes, she fancied she saw a spectre, for before her stood Mr. Swammer in a handsome sable-fur, with gold buckles, his hat on his head, his stick in his hand.

“My good Mistress Alina,” he said, “I must go out on important business, and perhaps may not return for many hours. Take care, therefore, that there is no noise on my floor, and that no one ventures to enter my room. A lady of rank, and﻿—I may tell you﻿—a very handsome princess, has taken refuge with me. Long ago, at the court of her father, I was her governor; therefore she has confidence in me, and I must and will protect her against all evil machinations. I tell you this, Mistress Alina, that you may show the lady the respect which belongs to her rank. With Mr. Tyss’s permission she will be waited on by you, for which attendance you will be royally rewarded, provided you are silent, and do not betray the princess’ abode to anyone.” So saying, Mr. Swammer had immediately gone off.

===Chinese===
厌世的斯瓦默竟有这般举动，实在太过反常，阿莉娜忍不住贴着房门偷听，还凑在锁孔上窥了几眼。她看见他站在房间正中央，正对小姐说着话，说得那样恳切、那样沉痛，连她自己都落了泪——尽管他讲的是外国话，她一个字也没听懂。她左思右想，只能认定老先生是在苦苦劝那位小姐重归美德之路，因为他的言辞越来越激昂，到后来小姐终于双膝跪倒，无比谦卑地吻了他的手：她甚至还落了几滴眼泪。于是他非常和蔼地把她扶起来，吻了吻她的额头——为此他不得不把腰弯得够呛——然后把她搀到一把扶手椅上。接着他张罗着生火，取来几味香料，就她所能辨出的，开始煨一壶香料酒。不巧老太太那时刚撮了一撮鼻烟，大声打了个喷嚏；斯瓦默立即把手臂直指房门，用一个直透她骨髓的可怕嗓音喝道："滚开，偷听的撒旦！"她自己也不知道是怎么逃回床上去的；到了早晨睁开眼睛，她还只道是见了鬼：斯瓦默先生就站在她面前，穿着镶金扣的漂亮貂皮大衣，头上戴着帽子，手里拄着手杖。

"好心的阿莉娜太太，"他说，"我要出去办一件要紧的事，兴许许多个钟头回不来。因此，留神别让我这层楼上有半点喧哗，也别让任何人斗胆踏进我的房间。一位有身份的小姐——不妨告诉你——一位非常美丽的公主，到我这里避难来了。从前，在她父王的宫廷里，我是她的太傅；所以她信得过我，而我也必须、并且一定要保护她，使她免遭一切奸计的毒手。我把这些告诉你，阿莉娜太太，是要你拿出合乎她身份的敬重来对待这位小姐。经蒂斯先生允许，将由你来伺候她；只要你能守口如瓶，不向任何人泄露公主的落脚处，这份差事你自会得到王家的重赏。"说完这话，斯瓦默先生便立刻走了。

===Original===
Peregrine now asked the old woman, if it did not seem strange that the lady, whom he could swear he met at the bookbinder’s, should be a princess, seeking refuge with old Swammer? But she protested that she believed his words rather than her own eyes, and was therefore of opinion that all which had happened at the bookbinder’s or in the chamber was either a magical illusion, or that the terror and anxiety of the flight had led the princess into so strange an adventure. For the rest, she would soon learn all from the lady herself.

“But,” objected Peregrine, in reality only to continue the conversation about the lady, “but where is the suspicion, the evil opinion, you had of her yesterday?”

“Ah,” replied the old woman simpering, “that is all over. One need only look at the dear creature to be convinced she is a princess, and as beautiful withal as ever was a princess. When Swammer had gone, I could not help looking to see what she was about, and peeping a little through the keyhole. There she lay stretched out upon the sofa, her angel head leaning upon her hand, so that the raven locks poured through the little white fingers, a beautiful sight! Her dress was of silver tissue, through which the bosom and the arms were visible, and on her feet she had golden slippers. One had fallen off, and showed that she wore no stockings, so that the naked foot peeped forth from under the garments. But, my good Mr. Tyss, she is no doubt still lying on the sofa, and if you will take the trouble of peeping through the keyhole﻿—”

===Chinese===
佩雷格林于是问老太太：那位小姐——他敢起誓，分明是他在装订匠铺子里遇见的——竟会是一位公主，来投奔老斯瓦默避难，这难道不奇怪吗？可她一口咬定，她宁肯相信他的话，也不信自己的眼睛；所以依她看，无论是发生在装订匠铺子里的事，还是发生在他房间里的事，若不是魔法的幻象，便是公主在亡命奔逃的惊惶仓皇之中，才闯进了这么一桩奇怪的奇遇。至于其余种种，她很快就能从小姐本人那里打听个明白。

"可是，"佩雷格林反驳道——其实不过是为了把关于那位小姐的话头再续下去——"可是，你昨天对她抱的那些疑心、那些坏看法，都上哪儿去了？"

"嗐，"老太太抿着嘴假笑，"全都过去了。只要朝那可爱的人儿望上一眼，就准能认定她是位公主，而且美得呀，就跟公主历来该有的那么美。斯瓦默一走，我就忍不住要去看看她在做什么，便又凑着锁孔窥了窥。她直挺挺地躺在长沙发上，天使般的头颅枕着手，乌黑的鬈发从她小小的白指间披泻而下——多好看的一幅画呀！她穿着银纱的衣裙，薄纱底下胸脯和手臂隐约可见，脚上是一双金色的拖鞋。有一只掉了下来，露出她没有穿袜子，一只赤裸的脚从衣裳底下探出头来。可是，我的好蒂斯先生，这会儿她八成还躺在那张沙发上呢；您要是不嫌麻烦，就凑到锁孔上瞧一眼——"

===Original===
“What do you say?” interrupted Peregrine with vehemence, “What do you say? Shall I expose myself to her seductive sight, which might urge me into all manner of follies?”

“Courage, Peregrine! resist the temptation!” lisped a voice close beside him, which he instantly recognised for that of Master Flea.

The old woman laughed mysteriously, and after a few minutes’ silence said, “I will tell you the whole matter, as it seems to me. Whether the strange lady be a princess or not, thus much is certain, that she is of rank and rich, and that Mr. Swammer has taken up her cause warmly, and must have been long acquainted with her. And why did she run after you, dear Mr. Tyss? I say, because she is desperately in love with you, and love makes people blind and mad, and leads even princesses into the strangest and most inconsiderate follies. A gypsy prophesied to your late mother that you would one day be happy in a marriage when you least expected it. Now it is coming true.”

And with this the old woman began again describing how beautiful the lady looked. It may be easily supposed that Peregrine felt overwhelmed. At last he broke out with, “Silence, I pray you, of such things. The lady in love with me! How silly! how absurd!”

===Chinese===
"你说什么？"佩雷格林激烈地打断她，"你说什么？要我自己送到她那勾魂夺魄的眼前去，让她把我推进种种的荒唐事里去吗？"

"拿出勇气来，佩雷格林！抵制住这诱惑！"他身旁一个细声细气的声音念叨着，他立刻听出那是跳蚤大师的嗓音。

老太太神秘兮兮地笑了；沉默了几分钟之后，她说："我把整件事的底细给您说一说吧，照我看就是这么回事。那位陌生小姐是不是公主且不管她，有一点是肯定的：她出身高贵，又有钱财；斯瓦默先生热火朝天地揽下了她的事，同她也必定早就相识。再说，她为什么要追着您跑呢，亲爱的蒂斯先生？我说呀，因为她发了疯似的恋着您；爱情叫人眼瞎心疯，连公主也能被引进最离奇、最没脑筋的荒唐事里去。从前有个吉卜赛女人给您已故的母亲算过命，说您有一天会在最料想不到的时候，从一门亲事里得到幸福。如今这话要应验了。"

说完，老太太又絮絮叨叨地夸起那位小姐的模样何等标致。不难想见，佩雷格林只觉得脑子里嗡的一声。最后他终于发作了："求求你，别说这些了。那位小姐恋着我！多蠢！多荒唐！"

===Original===
“Umph!” said the old woman. “If that were not the case she would not have sighed so piteously, she would not have exclaimed so lamentably, ‘no, my dear Peregrine, my sweet friend, you will not, you cannot be cruel to me. I shall see you again, and enjoy all the happiness of heaven.’ And our old Mr. Swammer! she has quite changed him. Did I ever use to get anything of him but a paltry sixpence for a Christmas box? And now he gave me this morning a crown, with such a kind look﻿—no common thing with him﻿—as a douceur beforehand for my services to the lady. There’s something in it all. I’ll lay you anything that in the end Mr. Swammer is her ambassador to you.”

And again the old woman began to speak of the grace and loveliness of the lady with an animation that sounded strange enough in the mouth of a withered creature like herself, till Peregrine jumped up all fire and fury, and cried out like a madman, “Be it as it will﻿—down, down to the keyhole!” In vain he was warned by Master Flea, who sat in the neckcloth of the enamoured Peregrine, and had hid himself in a fold. Peregrine did not hear his voice, and Master Flea learned what he ought to have known long before: namely, that something may be done with the most obstinate man, but not with a lover.

===Chinese===
"哼！"老太太说，"要不是这样，她才不会叹得那么可怜，也不会哭得那么凄惨：'不，我亲爱的佩雷格林，我甜蜜的朋友，你不会、你也不能对我这么狠心。我还要见到你的，还要享尽天堂的一切幸福。'再说说我们的老斯瓦默先生！她可把他整个儿变了个样。往年到了圣诞，他给我的赏钱几时出过一枚寒碜的六便士？可今天早晨，他竟给了我一枚克朗（值五先令），还带着那么和善的眼色——在他可绝非寻常事——说是预先酬谢我伺候小姐的辛劳。这里头一定有名堂。我敢同您打任何赌：到头来，斯瓦默先生准是她在您跟前的全权大使。"

老太太又兴致勃勃地说起那位小姐的仪态风姿来；那股活跃劲儿，出自她这样一具干瘪的躯壳，听着实在够古怪的。佩雷格林终于按捺不住，满腔怒火地跳起来，疯子一般喊道："随它去吧——下楼，下楼看锁孔去！"跳蚤大师坐在热恋中的佩雷格林的领结里，把身子藏进一道褶缝，百般警告，全是徒劳。佩雷格林没有听见他的声音；跳蚤大师这才悟出一个他早该知道的道理：天底下最倔的汉子也总有法子可想，唯独对恋爱中的人没有办法。
===Original===
The lady did, indeed, lie on the sofa, just as the old woman had described, and Peregrine found that no mortal language was adequate to the expression of the heavenly charms which overspread the lovely figure. Her dress, of real silver tissue, with strange embroidery, was quite fantastic, and might do very well for the negligee of the princess Gamaheh, which she had perhaps worn in Famagusta, at the very moment of her being kissed to death by the malicious Leech-Prince. At all events it was so beautiful, and so exceedingly strange, that the idea of it could never have come from the head of the most genial theatrical tailor, nor have been conceived by the sublimest milliner.

“Yes, it is she! it is the Princess Gamaheh!” murmured Peregrine, trembling with anxiety and pleasure. But when the fair one sighed, “Peregrine! my Peregrine!” the full madness of the passion seized him, and it was only an unnameable anxiety, robbing him of all self-possession, that prevented him from breaking in the door, and throwing himself at the feet of the angel.

The friendly reader knows already how it was with the fascinations, the celestial beauty, of the little Dörtje Elverdink. The editor, however, may safely declare, that, after he too had peeped through the keyhole, and seen the fair one in her fantastic dress of tissue, he can say nothing more than that Dörtje Elverdink was a very pretty little puppet. But as no young man can possibly be in love for the first time with any but an angel, without her equal on earth, it may be allowed also to Mr. Peregrine Tyss to look upon Dörtje Elverdink as something celestial.

===Chinese===
那位小姐果然躺在长沙发上，正如老太太所描摹的那样；佩雷格林觉得，凡间的语言已不足以描画笼罩着那可爱身影的天界风姿。她那身真正的银纱衣裙，缀着奇异的绣纹，奇诡到了极点，拿去做加玛赫公主的家居便装再合适不过——说不定她正是穿着它，在法马古斯塔（Famagusta）被那歹毒的水蛭王子亲吻至将死的那一刻。无论如何，这衣裙美得出奇，也古怪得出奇，再有灵气的戏装裁缝也想不出这样的点子，最高妙的女帽时装师也构思不出这样的花样。

"是的，是她！是加玛赫公主！"佩雷格林喃喃低语，又是焦灼又是欢喜，浑身发颤。可当那美人儿叹息着唤出"佩雷格林！我的佩雷格林！"时，情欲的疯狂整个攫住了他；若不是一股无名的心悸夺走了他全部的自持，他早已撞开房门，扑倒在天使脚边了。

小小的多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克有着怎样的媚态、怎样的天仙之姿，好心的读者早已了然。不过，编者尽可以稳稳当当地宣称：他自己也凑到锁孔上窥看过那位身着奇幻纱裙的美人，而这之后他所能说的，无非是——多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克是一个非常漂亮的小玩偶。然而，年轻人头一回恋爱，所爱的对象必得是位天使、是人间无双的绝色，方才合乎规矩；那么，也应当容许佩雷格林·蒂斯先生把多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克看作一位天上的仙子。

===Original===
“Recollect yourself, my dear Mr. Tyss; think of your promise. You would never see the seductive Gamaheh again, and now I could put the microscopic glass into your eye, but without such help you must perceive that the malicious creature has long observed you, and that all she is doing is only deceit, to seduce you. Believe me, I mean it well with you.” So whispered Master Flea in the fold of his collar, but, whatever doubts might arise in Mr. Peregrine’s mind, he could not tear himself away from the fascinating sight of the little one, who knew well how to use the advantage of being supposed to fancy herself alone; flinging herself into all manner of voluptuous attitudes, she put the poor Peregrine quite beside himself.

He would most likely have been still fixed at the door, had it not been for a loud ringing, and Alina’s crying out that Swammer had returned. Upon this he hurried up the stairs into his chamber, where he gave himself up to his love thoughts, but with these thoughts returned the doubts which had been raised in his breast by the admonitions of Master Flea. There was, indeed, a flea in his ear, and he fell into all manner of disquieting meditations. He thought to himself, “Must I not believe that this lovely creature is the Princess Gamaheh, the daughter of a mighty king? But if this be the case, it is folly, madness, to aspire to the possession of so exalted a personage. Then too she has begged the surrender of a prisoner, on whom her life depends, and as this exactly agrees with what Master Flea has said, I can hardly doubt that all which I would interpret into affection for me is only a mean to subject me to her will. And yet to leave her! to lose her! That is hell! That is death!”

===Chinese===
"清醒些吧，我亲爱的蒂斯先生；想想你的诺言。你说过，再也不要见那迷人的加玛赫了。这会儿，我本可以把显微镜片嵌进你的眼睛；可即便没有这份助力，你也必定看得出来：那存心不良的家伙早就盯上你了，她做的这一套全是做戏，为的是勾引你。相信我，我是一番好意。"跳蚤大师在他衣领的褶缝里这样悄声劝诫；然而，任凭佩雷格林先生胸中升起怎样的疑云，他也没法把自己从那小人儿勾魂的景象上拽开；她深谙如何利用人家以为她独自一人的错觉，把身子摆出种种旖旎慵懒的姿态，弄得可怜的佩雷格林神魂颠倒，忘乎所以。

倘若不是一阵响亮的门铃声，加上阿莉娜高喊斯瓦默回来了，他多半到这会儿还钉在那扇门前呢。他这才匆匆奔上楼，回到自己房里，一头栽进爱情的冥想；可随着这些念头一同回来的，还有跳蚤大师的告诫在他胸中搅起的疑团。他耳朵里当真落进了一只跳蚤，于是坠入种种忐忑难安的沉思。他心里想道："我能不相信这可爱的人儿就是加玛赫公主、一位威严君王的女儿吗？可若当真如此，妄图占有这样一位尊贵的人物，便是愚妄，便是疯狂。况且，她恳求我交出一个囚徒，她的性命就系在那囚徒身上；而这恰恰同跳蚤大师所说的一字不差，我几乎不能怀疑：凡是被我解作她对我有情的地方，都不过是笼络我、教我听她摆布的手段。然而，要离开她！要失去她！那是地狱！那是死亡！"

===Original===
In these painful meditations he was disturbed by a modest knocking at his door, and the person who entered was none other than his lodger. The ancient Mr. Swammer, at other times a shrivelled, misanthropic, grumbling man, seemed suddenly to have become twenty years younger. His forehead was smooth, his eye animated, his mouth friendly: instead of the odious black periwig he wore his natural silver hair, and in the place of the dark-gray upper coat, he had on a sable, such as Aline had before described him. With a cheerful and even friendly mien, by no means usual with him, he came up to Peregrine, protesting that he did not wish to disturb his dear host in any occupation, but his duty as a lodger required that he should the first thing in the morning inform his landlord he had been under the necessity of giving refuge to a helpless damsel, who sought to escape from the tyranny of a cruel uncle, and would therefore pass some time in the house. For this he needed the permission of his kind host, which he now requested.

Involuntarily Peregrine inquired who the lady was, without reflecting that this in fact was the best question he could ask to get a clue to the strange mystery.

===Chinese===
这阵痛楚的沉思，被房门上一阵斯斯文文的叩击打断了；走进来的人不是别人，正是他的房客。年迈的斯瓦默先生——平日里一个干瘪枯瘦、厌世而满腹牢骚的人——仿佛陡然年轻了二十岁。他额头舒展，眼睛有神，嘴角含笑：头上不再是那顶可憎的黑色假发，而是他天然的银发；身上也不再是那件深灰色外衣，而是换了一件貂皮大衣，正如阿莉娜先前描摹过的那样。他带着一副开朗甚至友好的神色——在他绝非常态——走到佩雷格林跟前，声言自己绝不愿打扰亲爱的房东做任何事，只是房客的本分要求他：清早头一件事，就得向房东禀报，他不得已收留了一位无依无靠的少女；她为逃脱一位狠毒叔父的虐政前来投奔，要在这栋宅子里盘桓些时日。为此他需要好心的房东首肯，如今他就是来请求这份许可的。

佩雷格林不由自主地问起那位小姐是谁，全然不曾想到，这实在是他所能问出的、最有利于摸到那桩奇怪谜团线索的问话。

===Original===
“It is just and proper,” replied Swammer, “that the landlord should know whom he is lodging in his house. Learn then, my respected Mr. Tyss, that the damsel, who has taken refuge with me, is none other than the fair Hollandress, Dörtje Elverdink, niece of the celebrated Leeuwenhoek, who, as you know, gives here the wonderful microscopic exhibitions. Leeuwenhoek was once my friend, but I must acknowledge that he is a hard man, and uses my goddaughter cruelly. A violent affair, which took place yesterday, compelled the maiden to flight, and it seems natural enough that she should seek help and refuge with me.”

“Dörtje Elverdink!” said Peregrine, half dreaming, “Leeuwenhoek! Perhaps a descendant of the naturalist, Antonie van Leeuwenhoek, who made the celebrated microscopes.”

“That our Leeuwenhoek,” replied Swammer, smiling, “is a descendant of that celebrated man, I cannot exactly say, seeing that he is the celebrated man himself, and it is a mere fable that he was buried about two hundred years ago at Delft. Believe it, my dear Mr. Tyss, or else you might doubt that I am the renowned Swammerdam, although, for the sake of shortness and that I may not have to answer the questions of every curious blockhead, I call myself Swammer. Everyone maintains that I died in the year 1680, but you see, Mr. Tyss, that I stand before you alive and hearty, and that *I* am really *I*, I can prove even to the dullest, from my *Biblia Naturae*. You believe me, my worthy Mr. Tyss?”

===Chinese===
"房东理应知道、也合该知道，自家宅子里住的是什么人。"斯瓦默答道，"那么请听着，我尊敬的蒂斯先生：投奔我而来的那位少女，不是别人，正是美丽的荷兰姑娘多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克，大名鼎鼎的列文虎克的侄女——就是在此地操办神奇的显微镜展演的那位列文虎克，这您是知道的。列文虎克从前是我的朋友，但我必须承认，他是个狠心的人，狠心地作践我的教女。昨天出了一场凶险的事端，逼得少女亡命出逃；她到我这里来寻求救助和庇护，看来是再自然不过的了。"

"多尔特耶·埃尔弗丁克！"佩雷格林半梦半醒地说，"列文虎克！莫不是博物学家安东尼·范·列文虎克（Antonie van Leeuwenhoek）的后人，就是造出那些有名显微镜的那一位。"

"要说我们的这位列文虎克，"斯瓦默微笑着答道，"是那位名人的后人，这我可说不好；须知他本人就是那位名人，至于说什么约莫两百年前他已葬在代尔夫特（Delft），纯属无稽之谈。信了吧，亲爱的蒂斯先生；不然，您怕是连我就是那位大名鼎鼎的斯瓦默丹也要起疑了——尽管为了图个简便，也省得一一应付每个好奇蠢货的盘问，我才自称斯瓦默。人人都一口咬定，我死于1680年；可您瞧，蒂斯先生，我活生生、硬朗朗地站在您面前；至于「我」实实在在就是「我」本人，哪怕最迟钝的人，我也能凭我的《自然圣经》（*Biblia Naturae*）向他证明。您信了么，我可敬的蒂斯先生？"

===Original===
“Since a short time﻿—” said Mr. Tyss, in a tone that showed his mental perplexity, “Since a short time I have experienced so many wonders, that I should be in perpetual doubt, if the whole had not been a manifest subject of the senses. But now I believe everything, however wild and fantastic. It may be that you are the dead Jan Swammerdam, and, therefore, as a dead-alive, know more than other common men, but as to the flight of Dörtje Elverdink, or the Princess Gamaheh, or however else the lady may be called, you are in a monstrous error. Hear how the matter really happened.”

Peregrine now related quite calmly the adventure he had with the lady, her entrance into Lemmerhirt’s room, up to her reception with Mr. Swammer, who, when he had done replied, “It seems to me, as if all that you have been pleased to relate were nothing more than a singular, yet very pleasant, dream. I will, however, let that be, and request your friendship, which perhaps I may have much need of. Forget my morose conduct, and let us be more intimate.
